# Daily Summary: 2025-03-22

### SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMIRe -- Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15358v1)
- **Authors**: Thomas Pickard, Aline Villavicencio, Maggie Mi, Wei He, Dylan Phelps, Carolina Scarton, Marco Idiart
- **Abstract**: Idiomatic expressions present a unique challenge in NLP, as their meanings are often not directly inferable from their constituent words. Despite recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), idiomaticity remains a significant obstacle to robust semantic representation. We present datasets and tasks for SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMiRe (Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation), which challenges the community to assess and improve models' ability to interpret idiomatic expressions in multimodal contexts and in multiple languages. Participants competed in two subtasks: ranking images based on their alignment with idiomatic or literal meanings, and predicting the next image in a sequence. The most effective methods achieved human-level performance by leveraging pretrained LLMs and vision-language models in mixture-of-experts settings, with multiple queries used to smooth over the weaknesses in these models' representations of idiomaticity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMIRe paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AdMIRe, a new SemEval task focused on Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation. The task challenges NLP models to interpret idiomatic expressions within multimodal contexts (visual and visual-temporal) and across multiple languages (English and Brazilian Portuguese). The task features two subtasks: ranking images based on their alignment with idiomatic or literal meanings, and predicting the next image in a sequence depicting an idiomatic expression. The paper details the dataset construction process, including the selection of target compounds, annotation procedures for static images and image sequences, image generation using diffusion models, and caption generation using a vision-language model. The paper also describes the evaluation metrics used for each subtask and summarizes the approaches and results of participating systems, including the use of large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) in mixture-of-experts settings.  The most successful methods leveraged pre-trained LLMs and vision-language models in mixture-of-experts setups, using multiple queries to mitigate model weaknesses in representing idiomaticity. A human evaluation was also conducted as a comparative benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel task that addresses a recognized challenge in NLP: representing and understanding idiomatic expressions. The multimodal aspect (incorporating visual information) is a key strength, as it moves beyond traditional text-based idiomaticity detection and introduces a more complex and arguably more realistic scenario. The inclusion of two languages (English and Brazilian Portuguese) adds to the diversity and applicability of the task. However, there have been some prior efforts exploring visual idiomaticity, so the novelty is incremental rather than completely groundbreaking. Saakyan et al. (2025) and Yosef et al. (2023) provide precedent. The generation of datasets using diffusion models, while becoming more common, provides some technical novelty in the context of idiomatic language tasks.

*   **Significance:** The task addresses a fundamental limitation of current LLMs – their difficulty in consistently understanding figurative language. This is significant because it highlights the need for more robust semantic representations and impacts the effectiveness of LLMs in various downstream NLP applications.  The creation and release of a well-structured multimodal dataset are valuable contributions to the research community, allowing for further investigation and benchmarking. The analysis of participating systems provides insights into the strengths and weaknesses of different approaches, guiding future research directions. The focus on datasets and tasks designed to genuinely test the ability of models to 'understand' meaning, rather than to game superficial correlations, is a valuable aspect of the work. The exploration of both static image and visual-temporal sequence subtasks is a strength, enabling a broader assessment of models' capabilities.  The observed performance issues around LLMs and bias towards treating expressions as idiomatic *regardless* of context points to an interesting limitation and an avenue for further work.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined task and clear motivation.
    *   Comprehensive dataset construction process, detailed explanation of decisions and issues.
    *   Multimodal and multilingual aspects enhance the task's relevance and complexity.
    *   Thorough evaluation of participating systems, providing valuable insights into different approaches.
    *   Publicly available dataset facilitates future research.
    *   The human evaluation provides a valuable comparative baseline.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset size, while adequate, is relatively small for modern deep learning approaches. (The paper acknowledged this limitation).
    *   The reliance on diffusion models for image generation introduces potential biases and limitations.  While human curation was performed, a completely natural set of images may have improved the dataset.
    *   The image captions, while helpful for text-only models, might have diluted the multimodal aspect to some extent.  Perhaps providing multiple captions with varying levels of detail could have been explored.
    *   The paper does not deeply explore error analysis or qualitatively analyze model failures to understand specific challenges.
    *   The lack of diversity in the dataset, both in terms of the linguistic content and the image generation (as acknowledged), might limit the generalizability of the findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** The AdMIRe task has the potential to stimulate further research in multimodal idiomaticity representation and to contribute to the development of more robust and reliable NLP models. The publicly available dataset will be a valuable resource for the research community. The insights gained from the task can also inform the design of future SemEval challenges and other NLP benchmarks.

*   **Rationale for Score:** While the task is innovative and addresses a significant problem in NLP, the incremental nature of the novelty (building upon existing work in visual idiomaticity) and the acknowledged limitations related to dataset size and potential biases prevent a higher score. However, the task's relevance, comprehensiveness, and potential influence warrant a solid score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EfficientLLaVA:Generalizable Auto-Pruning for Large Vision-language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15369v1)
- **Authors**: Yinan Liang, Ziwei Wang, Xiuwei Xu, Jie Zhou, Jiwen Lu
- **Abstract**: While multimodal large language models demonstrate strong performance in complex reasoning tasks, they pose significant challenges related to model complexity during deployment, especially for resource-limited devices. In this paper, we propose an automatic pruning method for large vision-language models to enhance the efficiency of multimodal reasoning. Conventional methods rely on the training data of the original model to select the proper pruning ratio for different network components. However, these methods are impractical for large vision-language models due to the unaffordable search costs caused by web-scale training corpus. In contrast, our approach only leverages a small number of samples to search for the desired pruning policy by maximizing its generalization ability on unknown training data while maintaining the model accuracy, which enables the achievement of an optimal trade-off between accuracy and efficiency for large visual language models. Specifically, we formulate the generalization gap of the pruning strategy using the structural risk minimization principle. Based on both task performance and generalization capability, we iteratively search for the optimal pruning policy within a given search space and optimize the vision projector to evolve the search space with higher upper bound of performance. We conduct extensive experiments on the ScienceQA, Vizwiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench datasets for the task of visual question answering. Using only 64 samples for pruning policy search, EfficientLLaVA achieves an accuracy of 83.05% on ScienceQA, along with a $\times$ 1.8 speedup compared to the dense LLaVA-v1.5-7B model.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "EfficientLLaVA: Generalizable Auto-Pruning for Large Vision-language Models" introduces a novel automatic pruning method specifically designed to reduce the computational complexity of large vision-language models (LVLMs) for deployment on resource-constrained devices.  Unlike traditional pruning methods that rely heavily on the original model's extensive training data, EfficientLLaVA uses a small number of proxy samples to search for an optimal pruning policy.  The approach focuses on maximizing the generalization ability of the pruning policy to unknown training data using structural risk minimization (SRM).  The method iteratively searches for the optimal pruning policy within a given space and optimizes the vision projector to improve the overall performance upper bound.  Experiments on ScienceQA, Vizwiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench demonstrate that EfficientLLaVA achieves significant speedups with minimal accuracy loss compared to dense models and other pruning techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a significant and novel approach to pruning large vision-language models (LVLMs) for resource-constrained environments. The key innovations lie in:

1.  **Few-Shot Pruning with Generalization Focus:**  Traditional pruning methods often require extensive retraining or validation on large datasets.  EfficientLLaVA's ability to determine a good pruning policy with just a few samples is a significant departure and a substantial contribution.

2.  **Structural Risk Minimization for Pruning Policy:** The use of structural risk minimization (SRM) to guide the pruning policy search is a unique and effective strategy.  By explicitly targeting the generalization gap, the method avoids overfitting the proxy samples and produces a pruning policy that performs well on unseen data.

3.  **Search Space Evolution via Vision Projector Optimization:** The iterative refinement of the search space by optimizing the vision projector is a clever way to improve the upper bound of the potential performance of different pruning policies. This allows the pruning process to converge toward better performing areas of the pruning policy space.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work stems from addressing a critical challenge in deploying LVLMs: their massive size and computational requirements.  EfficientLLaVA offers a practical solution to this problem, making it possible to deploy these powerful models on mobile devices, robots, and other resource-limited platforms. This has implications for a wide range of applications, including:

*   **Mobile AI:** Enabling sophisticated vision-language understanding on smartphones.
*   **Robotics:** Facilitating more complex reasoning and interaction capabilities for robots.
*   **Autonomous Vehicles:** Enhancing the perception and decision-making abilities of autonomous vehicles.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results across multiple datasets (ScienceQA, Vizwiz, MM-vet, LLaVA-Bench) demonstrating the effectiveness of EfficientLLaVA. The comparisons against strong baselines like SparseGPT and LLM-Pruner further highlight the advantages of the proposed method.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:**  The paper clearly explains the technical details of EfficientLLaVA, including the formulation of the generalization gap, the search policy optimization, and the search space evolution process.
*   **Practical Relevance:**  The work directly addresses a pressing practical problem in deploying large AI models. The demonstrated speedups and minimal accuracy loss are particularly valuable.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Analysis of the Vision Projector's Impact:** While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of optimizing the vision projector for search space evolution, it could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of *why* this approach works. Understanding the specific properties of the projector that are being improved could provide further insights and guidance for future work.
*   **Computational Cost of Search Space Exploration:** Although the method reduces the dependency on large datasets, it would be beneficial to explicitly state the computational overhead associated with the evolutionary algorithm to discover better pruning policies within a large space with respect to the resources required for pruning policy search and space evolution.
*   **GPU Utilization Discussion:** The GPU utilization of the models after the pruning is not discussed. This would add to the practical value of the paper.

**Overall:**

EfficientLLaVA represents a significant advancement in the field of LVLM pruning. Its novel approach to few-shot pruning with a generalization focus makes it a highly valuable contribution. The SRM-based formulation and search space evolution technique are well-motivated and effectively demonstrated. Although there are some areas for further exploration, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, and it is likely to have a notable impact on the deployment of LVLMs in resource-constrained environments.

Score: 8.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CCDP: Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies with Guided Sampling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15386v1)
- **Authors**: Amirreza Razmjoo, Sylvain Calinon, Michael Gienger, Fan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Imitation Learning offers a promising approach to learn directly from data without requiring explicit models, simulations, or detailed task definitions. During inference, actions are sampled from the learned distribution and executed on the robot. However, sampled actions may fail for various reasons, and simply repeating the sampling step until a successful action is obtained can be inefficient. In this work, we propose an enhanced sampling strategy that refines the sampling distribution to avoid previously unsuccessful actions. We demonstrate that by solely utilizing data from successful demonstrations, our method can infer recovery actions without the need for additional exploratory behavior or a high-level controller. Furthermore, we leverage the concept of diffusion model decomposition to break down the primary problem (which may require long-horizon history to manage failures) into multiple smaller, more manageable sub-problems in learning, data collection, and inference, thereby enabling the system to adapt to variable failure counts. Our approach yields a low-level controller that dynamically adjusts its sampling space to improve efficiency when prior samples fall short. We validate our method across several tasks, including door opening with unknown directions, object manipulation, and button-searching scenarios, demonstrating that our approach outperforms traditional baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CCDP: Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies with Guided Sampling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a method called Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies (CCDP) for improving robot imitation learning, particularly in scenarios where actions may fail.  CCDP builds upon standard diffusion policy frameworks by conditioning the policy on key failure cases, allowing the robot to learn recovery behaviors directly from successful demonstrations. Unlike typical conditional models that guide the policy toward desired behaviors, CCDP steers the policy *away* from regions associated with unsuccessful outcomes. This is achieved through a composition of diffusion models, breaking down the problem into smaller, more manageable sub-problems and avoiding the need for complex data labeling or environment modeling. The method is validated across various tasks, demonstrating improved success rates compared to standard diffusion policies and a rejection sampling baseline.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific application of compositional diffusion models to the problem of failure recovery in imitation learning, specifically *without* requiring access to failed demonstrations during training or environment models. The key idea of using failure features as "negative guidance" to steer the policy away from problematic areas is also innovative. This is a departure from standard robust control approaches that attempt to learn a single, robust policy.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical challenge in robot learning – dealing with action failures in real-world scenarios where collecting data on failures is difficult or even dangerous.  The proposed solution is data-efficient, as it relies solely on successful demonstrations and avoids explicit environment modeling, making it more accessible for real-world robot applications. By conditioning the policy on failure features, the method handles longer action histories more effectively, further enhancing its robustness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-structured explanation of the method.
    *   Strong experimental validation across diverse tasks.
    *   Addresses a practically relevant problem.
    *   Data efficiency compared to methods relying on failed demonstrations or environment models.
    *   The decomposition of the diffusion policy into state, history, and failure components adds modularity and controllability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The manual definition of failure key features is a limitation. While the paper discusses different choices for these features, an autonomous method for learning these features would significantly enhance the method's usability.
    *   The current work does not address optimizing the combination weights of the sub-problems in the policy composition, which may affect the performance and stability.
    *   The paper acknowledges the instability of NOT operation-based approaches but does not discuss alternative composition methods for diffusion models. This is a missed opportunity for providing broader context to the chosen composition method.
    *   The comparison to DP* is somewhat unfair, as DP* requires task categorization, which is not strictly required for the core implementation of a standard DP.  A fairer baseline would be DP with rejection sampling (i.e., restarting sampling upon failure), which CCDP clearly outperforms, but would provide a stronger comparative point.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of imitation learning by demonstrating a practical and data-efficient approach to failure recovery.  The idea of "negative guidance" based on failure features could be adopted in other robot learning scenarios. The compositional approach to diffusion policies also opens new avenues for building more modular and controllable robot learning systems. The paper provides a new perspective to recovery strategy by composing diffused policy.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to failure recovery in imitation learning that addresses a practical challenge in robotics. While there are limitations, particularly regarding the manual definition of failure features and the lack of weight optimization, the core idea of using failure features as negative guidance and the compositional diffusion model framework are significant contributions.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a clear advancement in the field with a well-defined method and strong empirical results. However, the need for manually defined failure key features, the lack of a fully optimized composition strategy and a lack of comparison to fair baselines slightly tempers the overall impact. While the composition of the diffused policy is helpful, the paper is still limited and requires further exploration for various situations.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Adversarial Transferability on Vision Transformers via Forward Propagation Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15404v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Ren, Zhengyu Zhao, Chenhao Lin, Bo Yang, Lu Zhou, Zhe Liu, Chao Shen
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) have been widely applied in various computer vision and vision-language tasks. To gain insights into their robustness in practical scenarios, transferable adversarial examples on ViTs have been extensively studied. A typical approach to improving adversarial transferability is by refining the surrogate model. However, existing work on ViTs has restricted their surrogate refinement to backward propagation. In this work, we instead focus on Forward Propagation Refinement (FPR) and specifically refine two key modules of ViTs: attention maps and token embeddings. For attention maps, we propose Attention Map Diversification (AMD), which diversifies certain attention maps and also implicitly imposes beneficial gradient vanishing during backward propagation. For token embeddings, we propose Momentum Token Embedding (MTE), which accumulates historical token embeddings to stabilize the forward updates in both the Attention and MLP blocks. We conduct extensive experiments with adversarial examples transferred from ViTs to various CNNs and ViTs, demonstrating that our FPR outperforms the current best (backward) surrogate refinement by up to 7.0\% on average. We also validate its superiority against popular defenses and its compatibility with other transfer methods. Codes and appendix are available at https://github.com/RYC-98/FPR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Improving Adversarial Transferability on Vision Transformers via Forward Propagation Refinement":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of improving the transferability of adversarial examples crafted on Vision Transformers (ViTs). It proposes a Forward Propagation Refinement (FPR) technique, which, in contrast to previous methods focused on backward propagation (gradient refinement), refines the features during the *forward pass*.  FPR has two components:
1.  **Attention Map Diversification (AMD):** This method diversifies attention maps by introducing random weighting, mitigating overfitting to the surrogate model. This process also implicitly induces gradient vanishing during backward propagation.
2.  **Momentum Token Embedding (MTE):** Inspired by momentum distillation, MTE stabilizes the updates of token embeddings by accumulating historical embedding information, reducing the impact of local optima.

The authors conduct extensive experiments demonstrating that FPR outperforms existing (backward) surrogate refinement techniques and maintains its superiority against popular defenses. They also validate its compatibility with other transfer-based attack methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in shifting the focus of surrogate model refinement for adversarial transferability *from backward to forward propagation*. This is a significant departure from previous approaches, offering a new perspective on the problem. While the individual components (AMD and MTE) draw inspiration from existing techniques (random weighting, momentum distillation), their application and combination within the context of adversarial transferability for ViTs is novel. The observation that AMD can *implicitly* induce gradient vanishing, normally achieved via back-propagation manipulation, is also a significant novel element. However, it should be pointed out that some of the concepts such as AMD and the introduction of randomness in the forward propagation, are in some sense similar to previous dropout and attention dropout works, but with a more nuanced approach.

*   **Significance:** Enhancing the transferability of adversarial examples is a crucial problem in assessing and improving the robustness of machine learning models. ViTs are increasingly important, making their vulnerability and robustness key to investigate. This work has practical implications. The improvement in transferability achieved by FPR (up to 7.0%) translates to potentially more effective attacks against unseen models, which in turn helps with hardening of models in practice. Showing FPR's compatibility with existing transferability methods is an important positive finding, enabling combination to create even stronger attacks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-motivated, clearly stating the limitations of existing approaches and highlighting the importance of forward propagation refinement.
    *   The proposed FPR technique is well-defined and explained. The two components (AMD and MTE) are justified with insightful arguments.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough and comprehensive, covering diverse target models (ViTs and CNNs), defenses, and integration with other transfer techniques. Ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   Theoretical justification is provided for why AMD might induce gradient vanishing, linking it to existing findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper provides a theoretical justification for the connection between AMD and gradient vanishing, a more in-depth analysis, possibly involving visualizing or quantifying the change in gradients, could strengthen the argument.
    *   The selection of the index set 'I' requires fine-tuning. The paper suggests a heuristic method, but a more systematic or adaptive approach could be explored. An adaptive method for finding the best 'I' would improve the usability of the proposed technique. It would be nice to see a relationship between index set 'I' and the model architecture it is attacking to better generalize.
    * Some of the underlying intuitions behind the proposed solution need further grounding. For example, the effectiveness of AMD is explained by the argument that random weighting acts as a surrogate of unknown models, but this can be made more concrete with a further explanation of why that surrogate works particularly well in this space.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in adversarial transferability. It opens up a new avenue for surrogate model refinement by focusing on forward propagation. It can inspire new techniques for diversifying features and stabilizing updates during adversarial example generation, especially for ViTs. The specific techniques (AMD and MTE) could be adopted and adapted by other researchers.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of adversarial transferability for ViTs. The shift in focus to forward propagation refinement is a conceptual leap. The practical improvements achieved are substantial, and the comprehensive evaluation supports the claims. While some aspects of the theoretical justification and index set optimization could be strengthened, the overall quality, novelty, and potential impact of the paper justify a score of 7.5. The ideas in the paper are compelling and have the potential to generate additional improvements and insights into creating more robust models.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Visual Persona: Foundation Model for Full-Body Human Customization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15406v1)
- **Authors**: Jisu Nam, Soowon Son, Zhan Xu, Jing Shi, Difan Liu, Feng Liu, Aashish Misraa, Seungryong Kim, Yang Zhou
- **Abstract**: We introduce Visual Persona, a foundation model for text-to-image full-body human customization that, given a single in-the-wild human image, generates diverse images of the individual guided by text descriptions. Unlike prior methods that focus solely on preserving facial identity, our approach captures detailed full-body appearance, aligning with text descriptions for body structure and scene variations. Training this model requires large-scale paired human data, consisting of multiple images per individual with consistent full-body identities, which is notoriously difficult to obtain. To address this, we propose a data curation pipeline leveraging vision-language models to evaluate full-body appearance consistency, resulting in Visual Persona-500K, a dataset of 580k paired human images across 100k unique identities. For precise appearance transfer, we introduce a transformer encoder-decoder architecture adapted to a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model, which augments the input image into distinct body regions, encodes these regions as local appearance features, and projects them into dense identity embeddings independently to condition the diffusion model for synthesizing customized images. Visual Persona consistently surpasses existing approaches, generating high-quality, customized images from in-the-wild inputs. Extensive ablation studies validate design choices, and we demonstrate the versatility of Visual Persona across various downstream tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Visual Persona, a foundation model for full-body human customization from a single image and text descriptions. It addresses the challenge of obtaining large-scale paired human data for training such models by proposing a data curation pipeline leveraging vision-language models to ensure full-body appearance consistency, leading to the creation of the Visual Persona-500K dataset. The method employs a transformer encoder-decoder architecture to extract local appearance features from body regions and projects them into dense identity embeddings to guide a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model. This approach aims to preserve both full-body appearance and align with text descriptions, enabling applications like virtual try-on, human stylization, and character customization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several key aspects: The creation and leveraging of the Visual Persona-500K dataset. The approach to full-body human customization as opposed to primarily facial customization. Use of vision language models for data curation. The architecture, which uses the encoder-decoder and incorporates this information to the identity embedding to maintain full body appearance details.

*   **Significance:** The ability to customize full-body human appearances in images has numerous applications across various industries, including fashion, entertainment, and virtual/augmented reality. The curated dataset of Visual Persona and the proposed model contribute to this field by addressing limitations in existing approaches that often focus on face images or single-image based learning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The Visual Persona-500K dataset is a significant resource for the community as a large-scale paired human data with full-body consistency is hard to obtain. This dataset addresses a major bottleneck in full-body human customization research.
    *   **Architectural Innovation:** The transformer encoder-decoder architecture adapted for a pre-trained T2I diffusion model effectively transfers appearance from input to customized images, which is demonstrated through empirical results. It overcomes the limitations of global-embedding approaches by encoding local appearance features, especially considering the full body.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents thorough evaluations, including GPT-based and human evaluations, demonstrating the superior performance of Visual Persona compared to existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on pre-trained models:** Reliance on pre-trained diffusion models (SDXL) and vision transformers may limit the model's ability to generate images beyond SDXL's inherent capabilities, as well as inherit some of its limitations (e.g., the potential for biased content generation).
    *   **Occurrences of Body Inconsistencies** The limitation as written addresses challenges with the pre-trained models. It doesn't address challenges specific to the model introduced in the current paper that could arise based on dataset, model architecture, etc. A discussion on the trade-offs made to get good results (e.g., architecture choices) would be useful.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Visual Persona-500K dataset and the full-body customization capabilities of the Visual Persona model could serve as a foundation for future research in the field. The data curation pipeline can be used as a blueprint for creating other paired datasets in different domains. The local-feature embedding and transformer-based architecture could be extended and adapted to other customized image generation tasks.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel approach to a challenging problem with significant practical applications, and provides a valuable dataset. It improves upon existing face-centered approaches by performing full-body customisation and introduces a robust architecture to preserve fine-grained appearance. It thoroughly validates this new method with a mixture of metrics and both human and GPT based evaluation. Its reliance on a generative model and therefore reliance on a single architecture choice (and potential for body inaccuracies) and limited scope of downstream task evaluations keep it from being a truly outstanding and groundbreaking contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Visual Position Prompt for MLLM based Visual Grounding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15426v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Tang, Yanpeng Sun, Qinying Gu, Zechao Li
- **Abstract**: Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at various image-related tasks, they encounter challenges in precisely aligning coordinates with spatial information within images, particularly in position-aware tasks such as visual grounding. This limitation arises from two key factors. First, MLLMs lack explicit spatial references, making it difficult to associate textual descriptions with precise image locations. Second, their feature extraction processes prioritize global context over fine-grained spatial details, leading to weak localization capability. To address this issue, we introduce VPP-LLaVA, an MLLM equipped with Visual Position Prompt (VPP) to improve its grounding capability. VPP-LLaVA integrates two complementary mechanisms. The global VPP overlays learnable, axis-like embeddings onto the input image to provide structured spatial cues. The local VPP focuses on fine-grained localization by incorporating position-aware queries, which suggests probable object locations. We also introduce a VPP-SFT dataset with 0.6M samples, consolidating high-quality visual grounding data into a compact format for efficient model training. Training on this dataset with VPP enhances the model's performance, achieving state-of-the-art results on standard grounding benchmarks despite using fewer training samples compared to other MLLMs like MiniGPT-v2, which rely on much larger datasets ($\sim$21M samples). The code and VPP-SFT dataset will be available at https://github.com/WayneTomas/VPP-LLaVA upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VPP-LLaVA, a multimodal large language model (MLLM) designed to improve visual grounding, which is the ability to precisely locate objects within an image based on textual descriptions. The authors address the challenges MLLMs face in accurately aligning coordinates with spatial image information by introducing Visual Position Prompts (VPPs). VPP-LLaVA uses two mechanisms: global VPPs that overlay learnable, axis-like embeddings to provide structured spatial cues, and local VPPs that incorporate position-aware queries to suggest probable object locations.  The paper also introduces a VPP-SFT dataset with 0.6M samples for efficient model training. The results show that VPP-LLaVA achieves state-of-the-art results on standard grounding benchmarks despite using fewer training samples compared to other MLLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in its approach to improving visual grounding by explicitly injecting spatial information into the MLLM via the Visual Position Prompts (VPPs). The design of both global and local VPPs is a sound approach for addressing the limitations of MLLMs in accurately interpreting spatial data. The generation of the VPP-SFT dataset also facilitates training and improves the model's learning process. The explicit use of coordinate references is a straightforward and effective way to address the coordinate alignment issue that other approaches might address implicitly or through more complex mechanisms.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper comes from the demonstration of state-of-the-art results with a relatively small training dataset. This suggests that explicitly guiding the MLLM with spatial information can be more effective than simply increasing the size of the training data. The VPP-SFT dataset is a valuable resource for the community. The improvements in visual grounding can be applied to a range of applications, including robotics, image segmentation, and human-computer interaction. The paper presents a viable approach for incorporating positional information into MLLMs. The ablation studies also contribute valuable insights into how different parts of VPP affect the grounding performance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the challenges of visual grounding in MLLMs.
*   **Effective Approach:** The VPP approach is straightforward, well-motivated, and effective in improving grounding accuracy.
*   **Strong Results:**  The model achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks with a relatively small training dataset.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes thorough ablations and qualitative results that provide insights into the effectiveness of different components.
*   **Useful Dataset:** The introduction of the VPP-SFT dataset is a valuable contribution.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in LLM Architecture:** The underlying LLM architecture is based on LLaVA-v1.5, so the novelty is concentrated in the VPP and training strategy rather than fundamental architectural changes.
*   **DETR Dependency:** The local VPP relies on DETR, which could be a limitation depending on the computational resources or desired performance characteristics.  While understandable, it ties the system to a specific object detection approach.
*   **Scope of Tasks:** The paper primarily focuses on visual grounding.  While region captioning results are presented, there is limited exploration of how the VPP approach might affect other MLLM tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of MLLMs and visual grounding. It provides a clean method for incorporating positional information into MLLMs, improves grounding accuracy and uses a reasonable amount of resources and a high quality dataset to achieve superior results. The paper's limitations lie in the reliance on an existing architecture (LLaVA), dependency on a DETR model for the local prompt and the lack of exploration of various tasks outside grounding and captioning. The strength and soundness of the method and its improvement of coordinate alignment for large language models warrants the following score:

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15451v1)
- **Authors**: Lixing Xiao, Shunlin Lu, Huaijin Pi, Ke Fan, Liang Pan, Yueer Zhou, Ziyong Feng, Xiaowei Zhou, Sida Peng, Jingbo Wang
- **Abstract**: This paper addresses the challenge of text-conditioned streaming motion generation, which requires us to predict the next-step human pose based on variable-length historical motions and incoming texts. Existing methods struggle to achieve streaming motion generation, e.g., diffusion models are constrained by pre-defined motion lengths, while GPT-based methods suffer from delayed response and error accumulation problem due to discretized non-causal tokenization. To solve these problems, we propose MotionStreamer, a novel framework that incorporates a continuous causal latent space into a probabilistic autoregressive model. The continuous latents mitigate information loss caused by discretization and effectively reduce error accumulation during long-term autoregressive generation. In addition, by establishing temporal causal dependencies between current and historical motion latents, our model fully utilizes the available information to achieve accurate online motion decoding. Experiments show that our method outperforms existing approaches while offering more applications, including multi-round generation, long-term generation, and dynamic motion composition. Project Page: https://zju3dv.github.io/MotionStreamer/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MotionStreamer, a novel framework for text-conditioned streaming motion generation. It tackles the challenges of incrementally predicting human poses based on variable-length historical motions and incoming text by integrating a continuous causal latent space into a probabilistic autoregressive model with a diffusion head. The method uses a causal temporal autoencoder (Causal TAE) to compress motion into a continuous latent space, avoiding the information loss and error accumulation problems associated with discrete tokenization methods. The model leverages temporal causal dependencies between motion latents for accurate online motion decoding.  To address error accumulation during autoregressive generation, the authors propose two training strategies: Two-forward training and Mixed Training. The system is evaluated on HumanML3D and BABEL datasets, demonstrating improved performance in text-to-motion, long-term motion generation, and new applications like multi-round generation, dynamic motion composition, and the capability of continuous stopping.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components. The integration of a diffusion model into an autoregressive streaming framework is a significant step forward. The Causal TAE is a novel solution for motion compression that addresses the limitations of previous tokenization-based methods by maintaining temporal causal dependencies and avoiding information loss due to discretization. This is very important for streaming motion generation. The two-forward and mixed training strategies, which address error accumulation, are also novel and appear to be effective. The continuous stopping condition, enabling the model to stop generation automatically, is also a meaningful contribution.

*   **Significance:** Streaming motion generation is a crucial task for real-time applications like video games, animation, and robotics. This paper tackles the limitations of current approaches and provides a promising solution. The improved performance on standard benchmarks, coupled with the demonstration of novel applications, underlines the significance of this work. The potential influence of MotionStreamer on real-time interactive applications is substantial. The project page further strengthens the reproducibility and accessibility of the work, encouraging the research community to build upon it.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a crucial and challenging problem in motion generation.
    *   Introduces a well-designed and novel framework with several innovative components (Causal TAE, training strategies, continuous stopping condition).
    *   Demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on established benchmarks.
    *   Showcases several compelling applications beyond standard text-to-motion tasks.
    *   Clearly articulated method and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the evaluation is comprehensive, the novelty of the individual components (e.g., diffusion model, TAE architecture) needs to be carefully considered. While *integrating* them in this specific causal streaming setup is novel, each component has its own established literature. It is important to acknowledge to what degree these models are being adapted or merely being used as building blocks in the novel framework.
    *   The gains in performance are notable but not necessarily groundbreaking. The improvements over some baselines may be marginal. In the future, a direct comparison against other more recent streaming techniques could provide additional insight.
    *   While the paper mentions limitations (e.g., unidirectional modeling), it would be beneficial to delve deeper into specific failure cases or scenarios where the method struggles.

*   **Justification for Score:**

Given the novel framework, the solution's impact on the field, state-of-the-art results, and the compelling application demonstrations, a score between 7 and 8 seems appropriate. The approach addresses limitations of previous methods, particularly in streaming contexts, which is valuable to the community.

Given the potential real-time applications, the thorough experimentation, and considering the minor weaknesses stated above (e.g. limited incremental increase over existing methods, and the fact that individual components can be seen as adaptations of established techniques), a final score of 7 reflects a solid contribution with significant potential for real-world impact and influence on future research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O: Distilling Masked Diffusion Models into One-step Generator
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15457v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanzhi Zhu, Xi Wang, Stéphane Lathuilière, Vicky Kalogeiton
- **Abstract**: Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) have emerged as a powerful generative modeling technique. Despite their remarkable results, they typically suffer from slow inference with several steps. In this paper, we propose Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O, a novel approach that distills masked diffusion models into a one-step generator. Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O addresses two key challenges: (1) the intractability of using intermediate-step information for one-step generation, which we solve through token-level distribution matching that optimizes model output logits by an 'on-policy framework' with the help of an auxiliary model; and (2) the lack of entropy in the initial distribution, which we address through a token initialization strategy that injects randomness while maintaining similarity to teacher training distribution. We show Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O's effectiveness on both class-conditional and text-conditional image generation, impressively achieving performance competitive to multi-step teacher outputs while drastically reducing inference time. To our knowledge, we are the first to successfully achieve one-step distillation of masked diffusion models and the first to apply discrete distillation to text-to-image generation, opening new paths for efficient generative modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents Di[M]O, a novel approach for distilling Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) into one-step generators. This addresses the slow inference speed of MDMs, a significant bottleneck in their application.  Di[M]O tackles two primary challenges: 1) The difficulty in leveraging intermediate-step information during one-step generation, which they solve by matching token-level distributions using an "on-policy" framework with an auxiliary model, and 2) The lack of entropy in the initial MDM distribution, addressed through a token initialization strategy that injects randomness while preserving similarity to the teacher's training data. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of Di[M]O on class-conditional and text-conditional image generation tasks, achieving performance comparable to multi-step teacher models with a drastically reduced inference time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in successfully achieving one-step distillation for *discrete* masked diffusion models.  Previous distillation efforts have focused primarily on continuous diffusion models, which have a PF-ODE and score function that MDMs lack.  The token-level distribution matching is also a novel solution for this specific distillation task. The initialization strategy also demonstrates novelty for tackling the challenge of initial entropy from the original setup.

*   **Significance:** This research is significant because it directly addresses a major limitation of MDMs: their slow inference speed. One-step generation makes MDMs more practical for real-time applications and resource-constrained environments. The approach opens up new avenues for efficient generative modeling, which has broad implications across various domains. Furthermore, this is among the first works to explore distilling MDMs for text-to-image generation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges specific to distilling MDMs, which is essential given the differences from continuous diffusion models. The proposed solutions are well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct extensive experiments on both class-conditional (ImageNet) and text-conditional (LAION-Aesthetics-6+) image generation tasks. The use of multiple metrics (FID, IS, HPSv2, Geneval) provides a robust evaluation of the generated images.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are well-designed and provide insights into the contribution of each component of Di[M]O (initial mask ratio, Jeffrey Coefficient, Gaussian Perturbation).
    *   **Qualitative Results:** The visual results demonstrate the ability of Di[M]O to generate high-quality images comparable to the teacher model.
    *   **Thoroughness**: The paper provides comprehensive implementation details and additional appendices further strengthening the validity of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scope of Evaluation:** While the quantitative metrics are strong, the evaluation is centered around image generation. Further evaluation across other MDM applications such as protein design or audio synthesis would further validate the generalizability of Di[M]O.
    *   **Dependency on Teacher Model:** The method relies heavily on the teacher model's capabilities. It would be interesting to see how it performs on different architectures.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of generative modeling. It provides a practical solution for deploying MDMs in real-world applications. The token-level distillation approach and the initial randomization strategy could inspire further research on efficient and effective distillation techniques for discrete data generation.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper addresses an important problem in a novel and well-executed manner. The results demonstrate the effectiveness of Di[M]O in achieving one-step generation for MDMs with competitive performance. While the evaluation could be broader, the paper provides a significant step forward in making MDMs more practical and accessible. The work will likely stimulate further research in distillation for MDMs and other discrete generative models.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### From 1,000,000 Users to Every User: Scaling Up Personalized Preference for User-level Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15463v1)
- **Authors**: Jia-Nan Li, Jian Guan, Songhao Wu, Wei Wu, Rui Yan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have traditionally been aligned through one-size-fits-all approaches that assume uniform human preferences, fundamentally overlooking the diversity in user values and needs. This paper introduces a comprehensive framework for scalable personalized alignment of LLMs. We establish a systematic preference space characterizing psychological and behavioral dimensions, alongside diverse persona representations for robust preference inference in real-world scenarios. Building upon this foundation, we introduce \textsc{AlignX}, a large-scale dataset of over 1.3 million personalized preference examples, and develop two complementary alignment approaches: \textit{in-context alignment} directly conditioning on persona representations and \textit{preference-bridged alignment} modeling intermediate preference distributions. Extensive experiments demonstrate substantial improvements over existing methods, with an average 17.06\% accuracy gain across four benchmarks while exhibiting a strong adaptation capability to novel preferences, robustness to limited user data, and precise preference controllability. These results validate our framework's effectiveness, advancing toward truly user-adaptive AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of current large language model (LLM) alignment techniques, which typically employ a one-size-fits-all approach that overlooks the inherent diversity in human preferences and values.  The authors propose a comprehensive framework for scalable personalized alignment. This framework includes: a systematic preference space that characterizes psychological and behavioral dimensions; diverse persona representations for inferring preferences; ALIGNX, a large-scale dataset with over 1.3 million personalized preference examples derived from forum data; and two complementary alignment approaches - in-context alignment (ICA) and preference-bridged alignment (PBA). ICA directly conditions on persona representations, while PBA models intermediate preference distributions.  Experiments demonstrate significant improvements over existing methods across several benchmarks, highlighting adaptation to novel preferences, robustness to limited user data, and precise preference controllability.  The authors argue that their framework represents a significant step towards truly user-adaptive AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel elements: the comprehensive preference space, the ALIGNX dataset, and the ALIGNXPERT architecture with its ICA and PBA components.  The ALIGNX dataset is a particularly important contribution, as it tackles the scarcity of personalized preference data, a key bottleneck in this area.  While some components build on existing methods (e.g., direct preference optimization), the integration of these elements into a cohesive framework for personalized alignment is novel. The use of forum data is a creative solution to the data scarcity problem. However, the core alignment methods (ICA and PBA) are conceptually extensions of existing alignment techniques.

**Significance:** The problem addressed is highly significant. As LLMs are deployed to increasingly diverse user bases, personalized alignment becomes crucial for user satisfaction, fairness, and reduced bias. The authors demonstrate compelling empirical results, showing substantial improvements in alignment accuracy, adaptation capability, robustness, and controllability. This suggests that the proposed framework is not just theoretically interesting, but also practically effective.  The paper also highlights the limitations of current alignment techniques that prioritize universal values over individual preferences.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The paper provides a well-structured and complete framework for personalized alignment, covering data collection, representation, and modeling.
*   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The ALIGNX dataset is a valuable resource for the research community.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experiments convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach compared to baselines. The detailed analysis of adaptation capabilities, robustness, and controllability further strengthens the empirical evaluation.
*   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The focus on personalized alignment addresses a critical need for LLMs serving diverse user populations.
*   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making it accessible to a broad audience.
*   **Acknowledges Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the limitations and ethical concerns associated with personalized AI alignment.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Methodological Contribution:** While the framework is novel, the core alignment methods (ICA and PBA) build upon existing techniques. The novelty lies primarily in their application and integration within the personalized alignment framework.
*   **Potential for Overfitting:** The ICA method appears prone to overfitting as the training size increases. This is a known issue in model training, but it remains an important point to consider.
*   **Dependence on Forum Data:** The reliance on forum data might introduce biases specific to those platforms. The preference space is well thought out, but it's ultimately limited by the biases found within the data it was created upon.
*   **Limited Generalizability:** The paper claims generalizability to unseen preferences and controllability, but its dataset and dimensions it is aligning might not reflect real-world preference spaces perfectly.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a significant advancement in the field of LLM alignment, specifically addressing the critical need for personalized alignment. The development of the ALIGNX dataset and the comprehensive framework provide a strong foundation for future research. The strong empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

However, the incremental nature of the methodological contributions (ICA and PBA being extensions of existing methods) and potential issues related to forum data and ICA's vulnerability to overfitting temper the overall impact. While the concept and framework are novel, the core mechanisms are evolutions.

**Score: 7.5**

The score reflects the paper's significant contribution to the field (addressing an important problem, providing a comprehensive framework and dataset, and demonstrating compelling empirical results), balanced by the incremental methodological advancements and potential limitations. The paper provides a solid foundation for future research in personalized LLM alignment, and warrants significant attention from the community.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FP4DiT: Towards Effective Floating Point Quantization for Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15465v1)
- **Authors**: Ruichen Chen, Keith G. Mills, Di Niu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Models (DM) have revolutionized the text-to-image visual generation process. However, the large computational cost and model footprint of DMs hinders practical deployment, especially on edge devices. Post-training quantization (PTQ) is a lightweight method to alleviate these burdens without the need for training or fine-tuning. While recent DM PTQ methods achieve W4A8 on integer-based PTQ, two key limitations remain: First, while most existing DM PTQ methods evaluate on classical DMs like Stable Diffusion XL, 1.5 or earlier, which use convolutional U-Nets, newer Diffusion Transformer (DiT) models like the PixArt series, Hunyuan and others adopt fundamentally different transformer backbones to achieve superior image synthesis. Second, integer (INT) quantization is prevailing in DM PTQ but doesn't align well with the network weight and activation distribution, while Floating-Point Quantization (FPQ) is still under-investigated, yet it holds the potential to better align the weight and activation distributions in low-bit settings for DiT. In response, we introduce FP4DiT, a PTQ method that leverages FPQ to achieve W4A6 quantization. Specifically, we extend and generalize the Adaptive Rounding PTQ technique to adequately calibrate weight quantization for FPQ and demonstrate that DiT activations depend on input patch data, necessitating robust online activation quantization techniques. Experimental results demonstrate that FP4DiT outperforms integer-based PTQ at W4A6 and W4A8 precision and generates convincing visual content on PixArt-$\alpha$, PixArt-$\Sigma$ and Hunyuan in terms of several T2I metrics such as HPSv2 and CLIP.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "FP4DiT: Towards Effective Floating Point Quantization for Diffusion Transformers" addresses the challenge of deploying Diffusion Models (DM) efficiently on edge devices due to their high computational costs and large model sizes. While post-training quantization (PTQ), specifically integer quantization, has been previously employed to mitigate these issues, the authors identify limitations in existing methods that focus primarily on traditional convolutional U-Nets and do not adapt well to newer Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architectures. To overcome these limitations, the authors propose a novel method named FP4DiT, which utilizes Floating-Point Quantization (FPQ) rather than integer quantization. Their approach includes an extension of the Adaptive Rounding PTQ technique, focusing on robust online activation quantization that considers the variability in activation depending on the input data. Experimental results indicate that FP4DiT surpasses previous integer-based PTQ techniques in terms of visual output quality across several metrics, demonstrating its effectiveness in generating convincing visual content on multiple state-of-the-art datasets. ### Critical Evaluation: **Novelty and Contribution**: The proposal of FP4DiT is quite notable, as it introduces a new quantization method specifically tailored for Diffusion Transformers, an area that has not been thoroughly explored previously in the question of floating point quantization. The focus on adapting quantization techniques meant for traditional architectures to the needs of DiTs represents a significant step forward in the field. **Strengths**: 1. **Timeliness**: Given the rise of Diffusion Transformers in generating high-quality images, this research is relevant and addresses a pressing challenge in practical applications. 2. **Methodological Innovation**: The adaptation and enhancement of quantization techniques to suit the unique characteristics of DiTs indicates a thoughtful and innovative approach to the problem. 3. **Empirical Validation**: The authors provide empirical results that demonstrate the efficacy of their proposed method over existing techniques, which strengthens the paper's practical utility. **Weaknesses**: 1. **Scope of Evaluation**: The empirical tests, while promising, might benefit from a broader scope of evaluation across more diverse datasets or comparisons against other emerging techniques in the space of floating-point quantization that were not previously included. 2. **Discussions on Limitations**: The paper could delve deeper into potential limitations of FPQ in certain scenarios, such as how it might perform against extensive varied input distributions beyond the tested sets. **Potential Impact**: Given that the demand for efficient model deployment on edge devices is ever-increasing, the introduction of FP4DiT could significantly influence further research in quantization techniques, especially for deep learning models relying on transformer architectures. ### Score: 8 **Rationale**: The paper scores an 8 due to its solid novelty, timely relevance, and methodological contributions, which represent a substantial advancement in the quantization of advanced models. However, it falls short of a perfect score because of the limited scope of its evaluation and lack of broader context regarding potential limitations. Further research building on this foundation could yield even greater insights and validation within the field.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Cube: A Roblox View of 3D Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15475v1)
- **Authors**: Foundation AI Team, Kiran Bhat, Nishchaie Khanna, Karun Channa, Tinghui Zhou, Yiheng Zhu, Xiaoxia Sun, Charles Shang, Anirudh Sudarshan, Maurice Chu, Daiqing Li, Kangle Deng, Jean-Philippe Fauconnier, Tijmen Verhulsdonck, Maneesh Agrawala, Kayvon Fatahalian, Alexander Weiss, Christian Reiser, Ravi Kiran Chirravuri, Ravali Kandur, Alejandro Pelaez, Akash Garg, Michael Palleschi, Jessica Wang, Skylar Litz, Leon Liu, Anying Li, David Harmon, Derek Liu, Liangjun Feng, Denis Goupil, Lukas Kuczynski, Jihyun Yoon, Naveen Marri, Peiye Zhuang, Yinan Zhang, Brian Yin, Haomiao Jiang, Marcel van Workum, Thomas Lane, Bryce Erickson, Salil Pathare, Kyle Price, Anupam Singh, David Baszucki
- **Abstract**: Foundation models trained on vast amounts of data have demonstrated remarkable reasoning and generation capabilities in the domains of text, images, audio and video. Our goal at Roblox is to build such a foundation model for 3D intelligence, a model that can support developers in producing all aspects of a Roblox experience, from generating 3D objects and scenes to rigging characters for animation to producing programmatic scripts describing object behaviors. We discuss three key design requirements for such a 3D foundation model and then present our first step towards building such a model. We expect that 3D geometric shapes will be a core data type and describe our solution for 3D shape tokenizer. We show how our tokenization scheme can be used in applications for text-to-shape generation, shape-to-text generation and text-to-scene generation. We demonstrate how these applications can collaborate with existing large language models (LLMs) to perform scene analysis and reasoning. We conclude with a discussion outlining our path to building a fully unified foundation model for 3D intelligence.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Cube: A Roblox View of 3D Intelligence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a first step towards building a 3D intelligence foundation model at Roblox. It focuses primarily on 3D shape tokenization, which converts 3D shapes into discrete tokens. This is achieved through a pipeline involving point cloud sampling, a Perceiver-based transformer, optimal transport vector quantization (OptVQ), and a decoding process. The authors introduce techniques like phase-modulated positional encoding (PMPE) and stochastic gradient shortcuts to improve training stability and reconstruction quality.  The tokenization scheme is used in applications such as text-to-shape generation, shape-to-text generation, and text-to-scene generation. The applications can interact with LLMs for scene analysis. The paper emphasizes the need for a 3D foundation model to learn jointly from sparse multi-modal data, handle unbounded input/output sizes via an autoregressive model, and collaborate with humans and other AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Application:** The paper presents a tangible, concrete step toward a larger goal within the specific context of Roblox. It's not purely theoretical; it aims to solve a practical problem of enabling developers to create 3D experiences.
    *   **Technical Contributions:** The proposed techniques for 3D shape tokenization, including PMPE and stochastic gradient shortcuts, seem technically sound and demonstrate improvements in reconstruction fidelity and training stability. The integration of optimal transport for vector quantization is also a good choice.
    *   **End-to-End System:** The paper doesn't just focus on tokenization in isolation. It shows how this tokenization scheme integrates into downstream applications like text-to-shape, shape-to-text, and scene generation, demonstrating its utility and versatility.
    *   **Multimodal Integration:** The integration with LLMs to perform scene analysis highlights the importance of multimodal reasoning.
    *   **Open Source:** Making the code and model weights available is a strong move, allowing the community to build on their work and validate their results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty:** While the techniques are interesting, the overall architecture is not entirely novel. The combination of point clouds, transformers, and vector quantization is a fairly common pattern.  The novelty primarily lies in the specific techniques used within this framework, specifically PMPE and the stochastic gradient shortcut, and their adaptation to 3D shapes.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** The improvements demonstrated might be incremental rather than breakthrough.  It is difficult to assess the qualitative quality of the results presented in the text-to-shape/scene generation sections.
    *   **Roblox-centric Focus:** The focus on Roblox is both a strength (as it provides a real-world application) and a weakness. The techniques may not be directly transferable or applicable to other 3D domains or applications without significant modifications.
    *   **Lack of rigorous comparison to alternative methods:** the paper only provides a comparison with Craftsman, and doesn't consider more recent approaches in generative 3D modeling.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper is a solid engineering effort that addresses a real-world problem. It pushes the boundaries of generative AI in the context of 3D model creation and world-building, which is a hot research area.
    *   The open-source release will undoubtedly spur further research and development, particularly within the Roblox community.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a practical solution to a challenging problem in 3D content generation within a specific context. While the overall architecture is not groundbreaking, the specific techniques (PMPE, stochastic gradient shortcut) are technically interesting, and the end-to-end system demonstrates the utility of the tokenization scheme. The clear focus on the needs of the Roblox community, combined with the release of code and models, makes this a worthwhile contribution. However, the limited scope of novelty and comparison, along with the Roblox-centric focus, prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution to the broader field of 3D deep learning.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### CAM-Seg: A Continuous-valued Embedding Approach for Semantic Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15617v1)
- **Authors**: Masud Ahmed, Zahid Hasan, Syed Arefinul Haque, Abu Zaher Md Faridee, Sanjay Purushotham, Suya You, Nirmalya Roy
- **Abstract**: Traditional transformer-based semantic segmentation relies on quantized embeddings. However, our analysis reveals that autoencoder accuracy on segmentation mask using quantized embeddings (e.g. VQ-VAE) is 8% lower than continuous-valued embeddings (e.g. KL-VAE). Motivated by this, we propose a continuous-valued embedding framework for semantic segmentation. By reformulating semantic mask generation as a continuous image-to-embedding diffusion process, our approach eliminates the need for discrete latent representations while preserving fine-grained spatial and semantic details. Our key contribution includes a diffusion-guided autoregressive transformer that learns a continuous semantic embedding space by modeling long-range dependencies in image features. Our framework contains a unified architecture combining a VAE encoder for continuous feature extraction, a diffusion-guided transformer for conditioned embedding generation, and a VAE decoder for semantic mask reconstruction. Our setting facilitates zero-shot domain adaptation capabilities enabled by the continuity of the embedding space. Experiments across diverse datasets (e.g., Cityscapes and domain-shifted variants) demonstrate state-of-the-art robustness to distribution shifts, including adverse weather (e.g., fog, snow) and viewpoint variations. Our model also exhibits strong noise resilience, achieving robust performance ($\approx$ 95% AP compared to baseline) under gaussian noise, moderate motion blur, and moderate brightness/contrast variations, while experiencing only a moderate impact ($\approx$ 90% AP compared to baseline) from 50% salt and pepper noise, saturation and hue shifts. Code available: https://github.com/mahmed10/CAMSS.git
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CAM-Seg paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CAM-Seg, a novel continuous-valued embedding approach for semantic image generation.  It addresses limitations of traditional transformer-based semantic segmentation that relies on quantized embeddings, which the authors argue introduce information loss and hinder the capture of fine-grained details. CAM-Seg uses a VAE encoder for continuous feature extraction, a diffusion-guided transformer to learn a continuous semantic embedding space by modeling long-range dependencies, and a VAE decoder for semantic mask reconstruction.  The framework is trained using a diffusion loss and claims to facilitate zero-shot domain adaptation and robustness to various image degradations. Experiments on datasets like Cityscapes, SemanticKITTI, ACDC, and CADEdgeTune demonstrate improved performance and robustness compared to methods using quantized embeddings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in using continuous-valued embeddings *with* a diffusion-guided transformer for semantic segmentation. While continuous embeddings and diffusion models are not new individually, their combination within this specific architecture for semantic segmentation appears to be a relatively fresh approach.  The integration of bidirectional attention (inspired by MAE) into the transformer for enhanced feature learning is another incremental contribution. The use of pre-trained autoencoders to reduce the training burden of the overall system is a practical, but not particularly novel, design choice. The claims of superior zero-shot domain adaptation and noise robustness are notable, but require scrutiny in light of existing domain adaptation and robust segmentation techniques.

*   **Significance:**  If the claims of improved performance *and* robustness, especially in zero-shot settings, hold true, the paper could have a significant impact. The ability to generate more detailed and accurate segmentation maps, particularly in challenging conditions (adverse weather, viewpoint changes), is valuable for various applications like autonomous driving, robotics, and remote sensing. The potential to generalize across diverse environments without retraining is a key advantage. However, the significance hinges on whether the reported improvements are substantial compared to existing state-of-the-art methods in domain adaptation and robust segmentation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The architectural design combines established techniques (VAEs, transformers, diffusion models) in a relatively novel and potentially effective way.
    *   The experiments cover a range of datasets and conditions, providing a reasonably comprehensive evaluation.
    *   The ablation studies offer some insight into the impact of different design choices (input image size).
    *   The qualitative results showcase the fine-grained detail capture of this method (better than VQ-VAE baselines)

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The "no directly comparable approaches" statement prevents a direct, apples-to-apples comparison with existing domain adaptation and robust segmentation techniques. This makes it difficult to assess the *true* significance of the reported improvements. Without more competitive baselines (beyond the internal VQ-VAE and KL-VAE baseline) its not clear if the performance is SOTA.
    *   The reliance on pre-trained autoencoders, while practical, might limit the overall adaptability of the system to radically different data domains. The choice of which pre-trained model becomes a bottleneck.
    *   The explanation of *why* the continuous embeddings lead to better generalization could be more detailed.  Is it simply preserving finer details, or are there other factors at play?
    *   Although the experimental results cover many adverse weather conditions and various image resolutions, but the evaluation is not comprehensive. Specifically, the work lack direct comparison to other few/zero-shot segmentation strategies.

*   **Justification for Score:**
    Given the incremental novelty (combination of techniques), the potential significance in robust and adaptable segmentation, and the identified strengths and weaknesses, a score of 6 seems appropriate. The paper presents a solid technical contribution with promising results, but the lack of direct comparisons to existing state-of-the-art domain adaptation and robust segmentation methods prevents a higher score. If the claims of superiority can be validated through rigorous benchmarking against more competitive baselines, the significance of the paper would increase considerably.
