### Uncertainty Quantification and Confidence Calibration in Large Language Models: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15850v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoou Liu, Tiejin Chen, Longchao Da, Chacha Chen, Zhen Lin, Hua Wei
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in text generation, reasoning, and decision-making, enabling their adoption in high-stakes domains such as healthcare, law, and transportation. However, their reliability is a major concern, as they often produce plausible but incorrect responses. Uncertainty quantification (UQ) enhances trustworthiness by estimating confidence in outputs, enabling risk mitigation and selective prediction. However, traditional UQ methods struggle with LLMs due to computational constraints and decoding inconsistencies. Moreover, LLMs introduce unique uncertainty sources, such as input ambiguity, reasoning path divergence, and decoding stochasticity, that extend beyond classical aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty. To address this, we introduce a new taxonomy that categorizes UQ methods based on computational efficiency and uncertainty dimensions (input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction uncertainty). We evaluate existing techniques, assess their real-world applicability, and identify open challenges, emphasizing the need for scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches to enhance LLM reliability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the survey paper on uncertainty quantification in large language models:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of uncertainty quantification (UQ) methods for large language models (LLMs).  It addresses the growing concern of LLM reliability and the need to estimate confidence in their outputs.  The paper introduces a novel taxonomy for categorizing UQ methods based on computational efficiency and uncertainty dimensions (input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction). It evaluates existing UQ techniques, assesses their real-world applicability, identifies open challenges, and emphasizes the importance of scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches to enhance LLM reliability. The survey bridges the gap between traditional UQ methods and the unique uncertainty sources introduced by LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's strength lies in its proposed taxonomy for uncertainty dimensions specific to LLMs. Decoupling uncertainty sources unique to LLMs from traditional ML contexts is a valuable contribution. This categorization helps to identify understudied areas like reasoning uncertainty, which is critical for multi-step tasks. Furthermore, it introduces unique ways of seeing error with LLM responses, incorporating input, reasoning, and parameter considerations.

*   **Significance:**  UQ in LLMs is a critical area for improving reliability, particularly in high-stakes applications. The survey effectively highlights the limitations of traditional UQ methods when applied to LLMs and outlines the new challenges introduced by these models. This motivates further research and development of tailored UQ techniques. The thorough overview of existing methods and identification of gaps in research makes it a valuable resource for researchers in the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of UQ methods.
    *   **Novel Taxonomy:**  The proposed taxonomy provides a clear and structured way to analyze UQ methods for LLMs.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper articulates the unique challenges of UQ for LLMs compared to traditional machine learning tasks.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** It discusses the real-world applicability of various UQ methods, considering computational efficiency and interpretability.
    *   **Identified Gaps:**  The paper identifies key areas for future research, such as the need for more scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** While the paper evaluates methods, it lacks substantial comparative empirical results using a standardized set of benchmarks across different UQ methods. A deeper comparative empirical analysis would significantly strengthen the claims and provide more practical guidance.
    *   **Depth of Method Analysis:** While the breadth is good, the depth of analysis of each method could be improved. A more critical comparison between methods within each category would be useful.
    *   **Future Directions:** While the future directions are outlined, some of the suggested areas of research are generic. More specific proposals for addressing the existing limitations could enhance the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The survey can serve as a valuable resource and roadmap for researchers working on UQ in LLMs. The clear taxonomy and identified challenges can help guide future research efforts and contribute to the development of more reliable and trustworthy LLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by providing a much-needed structured overview of UQ in the context of LLMs. The proposed taxonomy is novel and valuable, and the identified challenges are relevant and important. However, the lack of in-depth empirical comparison and specific future research directions slightly reduces the overall score.

### Zero-1-to-A: Zero-Shot One Image to Animatable Head Avatars Using Video Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15851v1)
- **Authors**: Zhou Zhenglin, Ma Fan, Fan Hehe, Chua Tat-Seng
- **Abstract**: Animatable head avatar generation typically requires extensive data for training. To reduce the data requirements, a natural solution is to leverage existing data-free static avatar generation methods, such as pre-trained diffusion models with score distillation sampling (SDS), which align avatars with pseudo ground-truth outputs from the diffusion model. However, directly distilling 4D avatars from video diffusion often leads to over-smooth results due to spatial and temporal inconsistencies in the generated video. To address this issue, we propose Zero-1-to-A, a robust method that synthesizes a spatial and temporal consistency dataset for 4D avatar reconstruction using the video diffusion model. Specifically, Zero-1-to-A iteratively constructs video datasets and optimizes animatable avatars in a progressive manner, ensuring that avatar quality increases smoothly and consistently throughout the learning process. This progressive learning involves two stages: (1) Spatial Consistency Learning fixes expressions and learns from front-to-side views, and (2) Temporal Consistency Learning fixes views and learns from relaxed to exaggerated expressions, generating 4D avatars in a simple-to-complex manner. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Zero-1-to-A improves fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed compared to existing diffusion-based methods, providing a solution for lifelike avatar creation. Code is publicly available at: https://github.com/ZhenglinZhou/Zero-1-to-A.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Zero-1-to-A," a novel method for generating animatable 4D head avatars from a single image using video diffusion models.  It addresses the problem of spatial and temporal inconsistencies that arise when directly distilling 4D avatars from video diffusion models, leading to over-smoothed results. Zero-1-to-A iteratively constructs a spatially and temporally consistent dataset and optimizes the animatable avatar in a progressive manner. This progressive learning includes: (1) Spatial Consistency Learning (fixing expressions and learning from front-to-side views), and (2) Temporal Consistency Learning (fixing views and learning from relaxed to exaggerated expressions). The method uses an updatable dataset, SymGEN, to cache video diffusion results and create a mutually beneficial relationship between avatar generation and dataset construction.  The authors demonstrate through experiments that Zero-1-to-A improves fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed compared to existing diffusion-based approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the symbiotic generation (SymGEN) and the progressive learning strategy.  SymGEN provides a method to refine and improve the training data used for avatar generation by incorporating the avatar itself into the video diffusion process, creating a feedback loop.  The progressive learning strategy, decoupling spatial and temporal consistency, is also a significant contribution, allowing for a smoother and more stable learning process. While using SDS and video diffusion models is not new in itself, the specific combination of these techniques with the progressive training scheme and SymGEN constitutes a novel approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation in data-free head avatar generation: spatial and temporal inconsistencies arising from using video diffusion models.  By generating more consistent and high-quality 4D avatars from a single image, the paper advances the state-of-the-art in this area. The improved fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed have potential applications in AR/VR, gaming, and other fields requiring lifelike digital human representations. However, it's important to note that the method still depends on pre-trained diffusion models, so improvements in those underlying models would likely translate into improvements in Zero-1-to-A.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Technical Contribution:** The SymGEN and progressive learning approach represent a genuine advance in addressing the challenges of distilling knowledge from video diffusion models for avatar creation.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper includes comprehensive experiments, including ablation studies that demonstrate the effectiveness of the different components of the method. The comparisons with other state-of-the-art methods (both quantitative and qualitative) support the claims made by the authors.
    *   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the method and the experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The approach relies heavily on the quality and capabilities of the pre-trained video diffusion models. While the method improves upon existing SDS techniques, its performance is ultimately bounded by the underlying diffusion model.
    *   **Limitations in extreme hair modeling**: The method struggles to model extreme hairstyles, primarily because it restricts gaussians to be aligned with the FLAME mesh.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research in data-free avatar generation, particularly in addressing consistency issues and improving the quality and efficiency of avatar creation. The progressive training method could be adapted to other generative tasks as well. The publicly available code should further promote adoption and experimentation with the technique.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to a challenging problem in data-free avatar generation and demonstrates its effectiveness through comprehensive experiments. The symbiotic generation and progressive learning strategy are technically sound contributions. The method is likely to be of significant interest to the computer vision and graphics communities. However, it's not a complete paradigm shift, and the limitations imposed by dependency on pre-trained diffusion models and the limitations in extreme hair modeling somewhat temper the impact.

### DroidTTP: Mapping Android Applications with TTP for Cyber Threat Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15866v1)
- **Authors**: Dincy R Arikkat, Vinod P., Rafidha Rehiman K. A., Serena Nicolazzo, Marco Arazzi, Antonino Nocera, Mauro Conti
- **Abstract**: The widespread adoption of Android devices for sensitive operations like banking and communication has made them prime targets for cyber threats, particularly Advanced Persistent Threats (APT) and sophisticated malware attacks. Traditional malware detection methods rely on binary classification, failing to provide insights into adversarial Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs). Understanding malware behavior is crucial for enhancing cybersecurity defenses. To address this gap, we introduce DroidTTP, a framework mapping Android malware behaviors to TTPs based on the MITRE ATT&CK framework. Our curated dataset explicitly links MITRE TTPs to Android applications. We developed an automated solution leveraging the Problem Transformation Approach (PTA) and Large Language Models (LLMs) to map applications to both Tactics and Techniques. Additionally, we employed Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with prompt engineering and LLM fine-tuning for TTP predictions. Our structured pipeline includes dataset creation, hyperparameter tuning, data augmentation, feature selection, model development, and SHAP-based model interpretability. Among LLMs, Llama achieved the highest performance in Tactic classification with a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9583 and Hamming Loss of 0.0182, and in Technique classification with a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9348 and Hamming Loss of 0.0127. However, the Label Powerset XGBoost model outperformed LLMs, achieving a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9893 for Tactic classification and 0.9753 for Technique classification, with a Hamming Loss of 0.0054 and 0.0050, respectively. While XGBoost showed superior performance, the narrow margin highlights the potential of LLM-based approaches in TTP classification.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "DroidTTP: Mapping Android Applications with TTP for Cyber Threat Intelligence" presents a novel solution, DroidTTP, to map Android malware behavior to Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs) defined by the MITRE ATT&CK framework. The authors address the gap in existing malware detection methods which primarily focus on binary classification and lack insights into attacker methodologies. DroidTTP includes the creation of a new dataset linking MITRE TTPs to Android applications, an automated solution leveraging Problem Transformation Approach (PTA) and Large Language Models (LLMs) for TTP mapping, and experiments with Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and LLM fine-tuning. The results show that Label Powerset XGBoost achieves the best performance for both Tactic and Technique classification, and that fine-tuned LLMs exhibit comparable performance, highlighting their potential in TTP classification.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel and valuable dataset. Also, the *combination* of applying PTA and LLMs to the problem of TTP mapping for Android malware exhibits a certain degree of novelty, however PTA and LLMs are established techniques in themselves. The enhanced feature selection strategy is also a valuable contribution. Using LLMs and RAG are not necessarily novel concepts, but their specific application and evaluation in this context adds some value.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a significant problem in Android malware analysis: moving beyond simple detection to understanding attacker behavior. By providing a system for mapping malware to TTPs, the paper empowers security analysts to develop more effective defensive strategies and perform better threat attribution. The creation of a specific Android-TTP dataset is a solid contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a critical gap in malware analysis.
    *   Introduces a novel dataset specifically tailored for Android malware TTP mapping.
    *   Explores and evaluates multiple methods, including PTA, RAG and fine-tuning LLMs.
    *   Includes thorough experimentation and analysis, evaluating the performance of various techniques.
    *   Investigates the use of SHAP values for model interpretability, providing insights into feature importance.
    *   Performance benchmark is provided, including comparative results between different approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance of RAG is quite low and may indicate less successful prompt engineering or relevance search.
    *   The limited availability of malware samples mapped to TTPs restricted the scope of their study. Though acknowledged, this remains a significant constraint.
    *   While LLMs are used and results reported, XGBoost (traditional ML) significantly outperformed the LLMs for many tasks. Thus, while potentially promising, their performance is not yet mature compared to more basic methods.
    *   The dataset construction methodology (manual review of references) is resource-intensive and potentially subject to bias.
    *   The dataset is relatively small.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in Android malware analysis, cyber threat intelligence, and the application of LLMs to security problems. The dataset and the DroidTTP system can serve as a valuable resource for further research and development. The detailed evaluation of different methods provides valuable insights for researchers and practitioners.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** Despite the innovative application of PTA and LLMs, there is still a significant potential for further refinement and expansion. Moreover, the relatively small size of the custom dataset and reliance on traditional ML as providing the best results, tempered by rating of potential impact. While it offers a novel dataset, and tackles an important problem, it uses mostly existing methods and only shows slight advantage from LLMs currently.

### TruthLens: Explainable DeepFake Detection for Face Manipulated and Fully Synthetic Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15867v1)
- **Authors**: Rohit Kundu, Athula Balachandran, Amit K. Roy-Chowdhury
- **Abstract**: Detecting DeepFakes has become a crucial research area as the widespread use of AI image generators enables the effortless creation of face-manipulated and fully synthetic content, yet existing methods are often limited to binary classification (real vs. fake) and lack interpretability. To address these challenges, we propose TruthLens, a novel and highly generalizable framework for DeepFake detection that not only determines whether an image is real or fake but also provides detailed textual reasoning for its predictions. Unlike traditional methods, TruthLens effectively handles both face-manipulated DeepFakes and fully AI-generated content while addressing fine-grained queries such as "Does the eyes/nose/mouth look real or fake?" The architecture of TruthLens combines the global contextual understanding of multimodal large language models like PaliGemma2 with the localized feature extraction capabilities of vision-only models like DINOv2. This hybrid design leverages the complementary strengths of both models, enabling robust detection of subtle manipulations while maintaining interpretability. Extensive experiments on diverse datasets demonstrate that TruthLens outperforms state-of-the-art methods in detection accuracy (by 2-14%) and explainability, in both in-domain and cross-data settings, generalizing effectively across traditional and emerging manipulation techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TruthLens: Explainable DeepFake Detection for Face Manipulated and Fully Synthetic Data":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TruthLens, a novel framework for explainable DeepFake detection. TruthLens aims to overcome the limitations of existing methods, which often provide only binary real/fake classification and lack interpretability. TruthLens combines a multimodal large language model (MLLM) (PaliGemma2) for global contextual understanding with a vision-only model (VOM) (DINOv2) for localized feature extraction. This hybrid approach allows the model to detect both face-manipulated DeepFakes and fully AI-generated content, and also provide detailed textual reasoning for its predictions in response to fine-grained queries like checking the realism of specific facial features.  The paper demonstrates that TruthLens outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both detection accuracy and explainability across diverse datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty of the paper lies in the integration of two complementary models (MLLM and VOM) to achieve both high accuracy and interpretability in DeepFake detection across a broader range of content types (face-manipulated and fully synthetic).  While using MLLMs for DeepFake detection is not entirely new, the specific architecture and training approach with the adapter module, along with the focus on generating textual explanations for *both* face-manipulated and entirely synthetic images, constitutes a significant contribution. The modular architecture that is based on interleaving and concatenating features from both VOM and MLLM can be a novel aspect too.

*   **Significance:** DeepFake detection is an increasingly crucial area given the proliferation of AI-generated content. The paper addresses a key limitation of existing methods: the lack of explainability. This aspect is vital for building trust in DeepFake detection systems and enabling humans to understand the basis for the classification.  The ability of TruthLens to handle both face-manipulated and fully AI-generated content enhances its practical utility in real-world scenarios. The authors also provide compelling empirical evidence demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach. Additionally, handling the nuanced queries adds value to understanding the system reasoning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating improved accuracy and explainability over existing methods.
    *   A well-designed architecture that leverages the strengths of both MLLMs and VOMs.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation across diverse datasets and manipulation techniques.
    *   Addresses the critical need for explainability in DeepFake detection systems.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of failure cases.  What types of DeepFakes still elude TruthLens's detection, and why?
    *   The quantitative evaluation of explainability is based on an LLM-as-a-judge mechanism, which may introduce its own biases and limitations. A human evaluation of the generated explanations would strengthen the findings.
    *   Limited ablation analysis. Although there is some analysis provided on the use of various adapters, more information on architectural choices and training aspects could be provided.

*   **Potential Influence:**  TruthLens has the potential to influence the direction of DeepFake detection research by highlighting the importance of explainability and the benefits of combining different types of models (MLLMs and VOMs). The hybrid design and the focus on generating fine-grained explanations could inspire new approaches to this problem. This work provides an initial framework of text based explanation that can be integrated with other explainability methods such as visual highlighting.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** TruthLens represents a significant advancement in DeepFake detection, primarily due to its emphasis on explainability and its ability to handle both face-manipulated and fully AI-generated content. The technical approach of combining MLLMs and VOMs is sound and yields impressive empirical results. However, limitations in the evaluation of the explainability and limited ablation analysis prevent it from reaching a higher score. While there has been other work using LLMs for this, the integration of DINOv2 with its localized feature extraction, and the explanation for both types of fake image, are contributions making it novel.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### UniCoRN: Latent Diffusion-based Unified Controllable Image Restoration Network across Multiple Degradations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15868v1)
- **Authors**: Debabrata Mandal, Soumitri Chattopadhyay, Guansen Tong, Praneeth Chakravarthula
- **Abstract**: Image restoration is essential for enhancing degraded images across computer vision tasks. However, most existing methods address only a single type of degradation (e.g., blur, noise, or haze) at a time, limiting their real-world applicability where multiple degradations often occur simultaneously. In this paper, we propose UniCoRN, a unified image restoration approach capable of handling multiple degradation types simultaneously using a multi-head diffusion model. Specifically, we uncover the potential of low-level visual cues extracted from images in guiding a controllable diffusion model for real-world image restoration and we design a multi-head control network adaptable via a mixture-of-experts strategy. We train our model without any prior assumption of specific degradations, through a smartly designed curriculum learning recipe. Additionally, we also introduce MetaRestore, a metalens imaging benchmark containing images with multiple degradations and artifacts. Extensive evaluations on several challenging datasets, including our benchmark, demonstrate that our method achieves significant performance gains and can robustly restore images with severe degradations. Project page: https://codejaeger.github.io/unicorn-gh
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the UNICORN paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UNICORN, a unified image restoration network built upon latent diffusion models. UNICORN aims to handle multiple, unknown image degradations simultaneously, a challenging problem in real-world image processing. The method leverages low-level visual cues to guide a controllable diffusion model, using a multi-head control network and a mixture-of-experts strategy. The authors also present a new metalens image benchmark called METARESTORE to specifically evaluate multi-degradation restoration performance. The experiments demonstrate UNICORN's superior performance compared to existing methods on both synthetic and real-world datasets, including the proposed benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the unified approach to image restoration using a controllable diffusion model guided by low-level visual cues tailored for multiple degradations.  Previous works have explored universal image restoration and controllable diffusion, but UNICORN's specific architecture, the use of low-level cues extracted via a MoE strategy combined with curriculum learning, and the introduction of the METARESTORE benchmark contribute incremental, yet useful advancements.  The use of a metalens benchmark specifically designed to test the robustness of multi-degradation image restoration methods is also novel. A slightly bigger impact would be to also integrate a text driven degradation parameter learning.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical relevance of handling multiple degradations simultaneously. Most existing methods focus on isolated degradation types, limiting their applicability to real-world images. UNICORN addresses this limitation and performs favourably, potentially impacting areas like autonomous vehicles, underwater imaging, and low-quality camera image enhancement. The METARESTORE dataset provides a valuable resource for the community to benchmark and compare multi-degradation image restoration techniques.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Unified approach: Addresses a significant gap in image restoration by handling multiple degradations simultaneously.
    *   Controllable diffusion: Leverages powerful generative priors for robust and high-fidelity restoration.
    *   Multi-head control network: Effectively integrates low-level visual cues to guide the restoration process.
    *   METARESTORE: The benchmark offers a more realistic and challenging evaluation platform.
    *   Strong results:  Demonstrates improved performance compared to existing methods on a range of datasets.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational cost: Diffusion models are inherently computationally expensive. Although the paper addresses this by comparing inference times and demonstrating superior performance relative to other diffusion models, the runtime might still be a concern for real-time applications.
    *   Complexity: The architecture is complex. Understanding the interplay between the MLCN, the TSU and the MoE adapter layers can be challenging.
    *   Hallucinations: While the paper mentions mitigating hallucinations, diffusion models remain susceptible to generating content not present in the original image. The evaluation of the hallucinations must also be explored on the METARESTORE images.
    *   Incremental improvements: The paper uses several existing components: stable diffusion, controllable diffusion, MoE layers, visual cue extraction. The novelty is in how these are combined.

**Justification for Score:**

The UNICORN paper presents a significant contribution to the field of image restoration. While relying on established techniques such as diffusion models, the integration of carefully selected, low-level visual cues through a mixture-of-experts control network in a single framework represents a non-trivial advancement. The introduction of the METARESTORE benchmark is also helpful.  The improvements over existing SoTA approaches are notable and demonstrate the practical viability of the approach.  However, the complexity of the architecture, the relatively high computational cost, and the potential for hallucinations prevents a higher rating.

- **Score**: 7/10

### MASH-VLM: Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through Disentangled Spatial-Temporal Representations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15871v1)
- **Authors**: Kyungho Bae, Jinhyung Kim, Sihaeng Lee, Soonyoung Lee, Gunhee Lee, Jinwoo Choi
- **Abstract**: In this work, we tackle action-scene hallucination in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs), where models incorrectly predict actions based on the scene context or scenes based on observed actions. We observe that existing Video-LLMs often suffer from action-scene hallucination due to two main factors. First, existing Video-LLMs intermingle spatial and temporal features by applying an attention operation across all tokens. Second, they use the standard Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE), which causes the text tokens to overemphasize certain types of tokens depending on their sequential orders. To address these issues, we introduce MASH-VLM, Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through disentangled spatial-temporal representations. Our approach includes two key innovations: (1) DST-attention, a novel attention mechanism that disentangles the spatial and temporal tokens within the LLM by using masked attention to restrict direct interactions between the spatial and temporal tokens; (2) Harmonic-RoPE, which extends the dimensionality of the positional IDs, allowing the spatial and temporal tokens to maintain balanced positions relative to the text tokens. To evaluate the action-scene hallucination in Video-LLMs, we introduce the UNSCENE benchmark with 1,320 videos and 4,078 QA pairs. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MASH-VLM achieves state-of-the-art results on the UNSCENE benchmark, as well as on existing video understanding benchmarks.
- **Summary**: The paper "MASH-VLM: Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through Disentangled Spatial-Temporal Representations" introduces a new approach to address action-scene hallucination in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). The core idea is to disentangle spatial and temporal representations within the LLM using two key innovations: DST-attention (a masked attention mechanism preventing direct interaction between spatial and temporal tokens) and Harmonic-RoPE (a rotary positional embedding that balances positional IDs for spatial and temporal tokens relative to text tokens).  To evaluate the proposed method, the authors introduce a new benchmark called UNSCENE, designed specifically to measure action-scene hallucination.  Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on UNSCENE and other existing video understanding benchmarks.

Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:

Novelty: The paper presents a novel approach to mitigate hallucination in video LLMs, a significant challenge in the field.  The DST-attention mechanism and Harmonic-RoPE embedding are clever ideas that address the intertwined nature of spatial and temporal features and positional encoding limitations. While some prior work has explored separating spatial and temporal features at the input level, disentangling these features *within* the LLM's attention mechanism is relatively novel. The introduction of the UNSCENE benchmark is also valuable for the community, as it provides a specific dataset focused on a particular type of hallucination.

Significance: Action-scene hallucination is a practical problem that limits the reliability of Video-LLMs in real-world applications. By effectively mitigating this issue, the proposed method enhances the trustworthiness of these models. The state-of-the-art performance on both the UNSCENE benchmark and other standard video understanding datasets demonstrates the effectiveness and generalizability of the approach.  The UNSCENE dataset itself will likely spur further research in this area. The paper provides both quantitative and qualitative evidence to support its claims.  However, the dependence on GPT-3.5 for evaluating open-ended generations introduces some subjectivity, but the adoption of a known and widely used method does help make the comparison more objective.  Furthermore, given the reliance on LLaVA as a starting point and the focus on instruction tuning, the core innovation lies in the attention mechanism and positional encoding. The paper doesn't fundamentally challenge the overall architecture of Video-LLMs.

Strengths:
*   Clear problem definition and motivation.
*   Well-designed and explained technical contributions (DST-attention and Harmonic-RoPE).
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on a new, relevant benchmark and existing datasets.
*   Both quantitative and qualitative results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.
*   Ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components.
*   The UNSCENE dataset fills a gap in the evaluation of Video-LLMs.

Weaknesses:
*   The evaluation of open-ended generation relies on GPT-3.5 scoring, which is subjective and not reproducible without access to GPT-3.5.
*   The core innovation focuses primarily on the attention mechanism and positional embeddings. While significant, the overall architecture builds upon existing Video-LLM frameworks (specifically, LLaVA).
*   The paper lacks a detailed discussion on the computational overhead incurred by DST-attention and Harmonic-RoPE, which is a crucial aspect for practical deployment.

Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by addressing a specific and important problem in Video-LLMs. The proposed method is technically sound, well-evaluated, and demonstrates promising results. The introduction of the UNSCENE benchmark is a significant contribution. However, the reliance on GPT-3.5 for evaluation and the relatively incremental nature of the architectural changes prevent a higher score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeepPsy-Agent: A Stage-Aware and Deep-Thinking Emotional Support Agent System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15876v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Chen, Zebing Sun
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces DeepPsy-Agent, an innovative psychological support system that combines the three-stage helping theory in psychology with deep learning techniques. The system consists of two core components: (1) a multi-stage response-capable dialogue model (\textit{deeppsy-chat}), which enhances reasoning capabilities through stage-awareness and deep-thinking analysis to generate high-quality responses; and (2) a real-time stage transition detection model that identifies contextual shifts to guide the dialogue towards more effective intervention stages. Based on 30,000 real psychological hotline conversations, we employ AI-simulated dialogues and expert re-annotation strategies to construct a high-quality multi-turn dialogue dataset. Experimental results demonstrate that DeepPsy-Agent outperforms general-purpose large language models (LLMs) in key metrics such as problem exposure completeness, cognitive restructuring success rate, and action adoption rate. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules, showing that stage information contributes 42.3\% to performance, while the deep-thinking module increases root-cause identification by 58.3\% and reduces ineffective suggestions by 72.1\%. This system addresses critical challenges in AI-based psychological support through dynamic dialogue management and deep reasoning, advancing intelligent mental health services.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepPsy-Agent, an emotional support agent that leverages a three-stage helping theory (Exploration-Insight-Action) and deep learning techniques. The system uses a multi-stage response-capable dialogue model (deeppsy-chat) and a real-time stage transition detection model.  DeepPsy-Agent integrates stage awareness and deep-thinking analysis to generate higher-quality responses. The system was trained on a dataset of 30,000 real psychological hotline conversations, enhanced with AI-simulated dialogues and expert re-annotation.  The experiments demonstrate that DeepPsy-Agent outperforms general-purpose LLMs in key metrics like problem exposure completeness, cognitive restructuring success rate, and action adoption rate. Ablation studies confirm the effectiveness of stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Integration of Psychological Theory:** A key strength is the explicit grounding of the system in Clara Hill's Exploration-Insight-Action model. This provides a structured framework for dialogue management, moving beyond purely data-driven approaches. This theoretical backing is important for building more reliable and helpful systems.
*   **Dynamic Stage Awareness:** The ability to dynamically adjust the dialogue strategy based on context is a significant improvement over systems that follow fixed processes. The hierarchical classification model for detecting stage transition signals contributes to this dynamic behavior.
*   **Deep Reasoning:**  The claim of enhanced reasoning capabilities through a Test-time scaling "slow thinking" mechanism is promising.  While the paper doesn't fully explain the inner workings of the "slow thinking" mechanism, if effectively implemented, it could enable more nuanced and context-aware responses.
*   **Dataset Enhancement:** Creating a high-quality dataset with AI simulation and expert re-annotation is crucial.  Using real psychological hotline conversations provides a strong foundation for training.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of the stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules.  Quantifying the impact of each component strengthens the claims about their effectiveness.
*   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results indicate a substantial performance improvement over LLMs in key metrics relevant to emotional support conversations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Detail on "Slow Thinking" Mechanism:** The paper mentions Test-time scaling's "slow thinking" but does not elaborate on how it's implemented or how it contributes to logical reasoning within the model. This is a significant gap, as it's a central claim of the work. Further clarification of this mechanism is needed.
*   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** While the system shows promise, the paper lacks a detailed discussion of the limitations of the proposed approach. Specifically, it should address potential ethical considerations and biases in the training data that might impact the system's behavior. What happens if the hotline data reflects systemic biases?
*   **Dataset Creation Process:** While the paper mentions AI-simulated dialogues and expert re-annotation, more details about this process would improve the rigor of the study. How was the AI simulation performed? What inter-annotator agreement was achieved during re-annotation?
*   **Overstated Claims:** While the results are promising, some claims, like "paradigm shift," seem overly enthusiastic. A more nuanced and humble assessment of the contributions would be more appropriate.
*   **No discussion of failure cases:** It would be useful to know more about the limitations of the approach in the case studies through demonstrating failure cases.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty of the paper lies in the integration of psychological theory (Hill's model) with a deep learning architecture for emotional support conversation, in particular the addition of the "slow thinking" approach. The stage-awareness mechanism and the dataset construction process are also contributions. The significance lies in addressing the limitations of existing LLM-based emotional support systems and offering a more structured and theoretically grounded approach. If the system consistently demonstrates improved empathy, reasoning, and action guidance in real-world scenarios, it could significantly advance the field of AI-based mental health support.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a promising approach to building emotional support agents by combining psychological theory with deep learning. The dynamic stage awareness and the "slow thinking" mechanism are interesting ideas.  The experimental results are encouraging, and the ablation studies provide evidence for the effectiveness of the key components.

However, the lack of detail on the "slow thinking" mechanism, the limited discussion of limitations and failure cases, and the potential for overstated claims prevent a higher score. More in-depth information and rigorous testing in real-world scenarios would significantly strengthen the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Repurposing 2D Diffusion Models with Gaussian Atlas for 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15877v1)
- **Authors**: Tiange Xiang, Kai Li, Chengjiang Long, Christian Häne, Peihong Guo, Scott Delp, Ehsan Adeli, Li Fei-Fei
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-image diffusion models have been driven by the increasing availability of paired 2D data. However, the development of 3D diffusion models has been hindered by the scarcity of high-quality 3D data, resulting in less competitive performance compared to their 2D counterparts. To address this challenge, we propose repurposing pre-trained 2D diffusion models for 3D object generation. We introduce Gaussian Atlas, a novel representation that utilizes dense 2D grids, enabling the fine-tuning of 2D diffusion models to generate 3D Gaussians. Our approach demonstrates successful transfer learning from a pre-trained 2D diffusion model to a 2D manifold flattened from 3D structures. To support model training, we compile GaussianVerse, a large-scale dataset comprising 205K high-quality 3D Gaussian fittings of various 3D objects. Our experimental results show that text-to-image diffusion models can be effectively adapted for 3D content generation, bridging the gap between 2D and 3D modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Repurposing 2D Diffusion Models with Gaussian Atlas for 3D Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited high-quality 3D data hindering the development of competitive 3D diffusion models.  It proposes a method to repurpose pre-trained 2D diffusion models for 3D object generation. The core idea is the "Gaussian Atlas," a novel 2D representation of 3D Gaussians, which facilitates fine-tuning 2D diffusion models to generate 3D objects. The method maps 3D Gaussians to a sphere, then uses equirectangular projection to flatten them into a 2D grid (the Atlas). The paper also introduces "GaussianVerse," a large-scale dataset of high-quality 3D Gaussian fittings to support training.  The experiments demonstrate that this approach allows effective transfer learning from 2D to 3D, yielding improved 3D generation results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The Gaussian Atlas representation is the primary source of novelty.  While using 2D diffusion models for 3D generation isn't entirely new (e.g., Triplane approaches), the specific method of mapping 3D Gaussians to a sphere and then flattening it into a 2D atlas for direct fine-tuning is a significant contribution. The construction of the GaussianVerse dataset also contributes positively. The method's simplicity is a strength, allowing 2D models to be used with minimal architectural changes.
*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial problem in 3D generative modeling: the lack of data. By successfully transferring knowledge from readily available 2D data, the work has the potential to significantly advance the field. The results show improved performance compared to several baseline methods, suggesting a practical benefit. The proposed method provides a path to leverage the massive progress in 2D diffusion models for 3D tasks, which can have far-reaching implications. The well-structured dataset and experiments further solidifies the work.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the data scarcity problem in 3D generation and motivates the approach well.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The Gaussian Atlas representation is conceptually simple and technically well-executed.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper provides thorough qualitative and quantitative comparisons to establish the effectiveness of the proposed method. The dataset construction is a significant contribution itself.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper details the implementation and training procedures, enhancing reproducibility. The release of the GaussianVerse dataset helps significantly here.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Sphere Mapping:** While effective, the choice of sphere mapping as the intermediate representation may introduce some distortion and limit the expressiveness for certain complex 3D shapes.
    *   **Potential Artifacts:** Methods of 2D-3D conversion always carry a risk of projection errors or other errors.
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained 2D Models:** The performance is intrinsically linked to the quality and biases of the pre-trained 2D diffusion model. It's not clear how the method would perform with different 2D model architectures or datasets.
*   **Impact:**
    *   The method could become a standard approach for leveraging 2D diffusion models for 3D generation, especially in data-limited scenarios.
    *   The GaussianVerse dataset will likely be a valuable resource for the research community.
    *   The approach could spur further research into novel 2D representations for 3D data.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper presents a significant and novel contribution in leveraging 2D knowledge for 3D generation, tackling a key limitation in the field. The Gaussian Atlas representation is technically sound and effectively bridges the gap between 2D and 3D. The comprehensive experiments and the creation of the GaussianVerse dataset add substantial value. The reliance on sphere mapping and dependence on the quality of pre-trained 2D models are minor limitations that do not detract from the overall value of the work.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Zero-Shot Image Recognition in Vision-Language Models through Human-like Concept Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15886v1)
- **Authors**: Hui Liu, Wenya Wang, Kecheng Chen, Jie Liu, Yibing Liu, Tiexin Qin, Peisong He, Xinghao Jiang, Haoliang Li
- **Abstract**: In zero-shot image recognition tasks, humans demonstrate remarkable flexibility in classifying unseen categories by composing known simpler concepts. However, existing vision-language models (VLMs), despite achieving significant progress through large-scale natural language supervision, often underperform in real-world applications because of sub-optimal prompt engineering and the inability to adapt effectively to target classes. To address these issues, we propose a Concept-guided Human-like Bayesian Reasoning (CHBR) framework. Grounded in Bayes' theorem, CHBR models the concept used in human image recognition as latent variables and formulates this task by summing across potential concepts, weighted by a prior distribution and a likelihood function. To tackle the intractable computation over an infinite concept space, we introduce an importance sampling algorithm that iteratively prompts large language models (LLMs) to generate discriminative concepts, emphasizing inter-class differences. We further propose three heuristic approaches involving Average Likelihood, Confidence Likelihood, and Test Time Augmentation (TTA) Likelihood, which dynamically refine the combination of concepts based on the test image. Extensive evaluations across fifteen datasets demonstrate that CHBR consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art zero-shot generalization methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel framework called Concept-guided Human-like Bayesian Reasoning (CHBR) to improve zero-shot image recognition in vision-language models (VLMs). CHBR is inspired by how humans recognize images by decomposing them into concepts. The framework models concepts as latent variables and formulates image classification as summing over potential concepts weighted by a prior distribution and a likelihood function. To handle the infinite concept space, an importance sampling algorithm is used to iteratively prompt large language models (LLMs) to generate discriminative concepts.  Three heuristic approaches are then used to refine the combination of concepts based on the test image: Average Likelihood, Confidence Likelihood, and Test Time Augmentation (TTA) Likelihood. The authors evaluate CHBR on fifteen datasets, demonstrating consistent improvements over existing zero-shot methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of incorporating human-like concept reasoning into VLMs for zero-shot recognition is novel. The paper doesn't just apply VLMs; it explicitly tries to mimic a cognitive process. The importance sampling algorithm driven by LLMs for concept generation is also a significant contribution.  While using LLMs to generate descriptions or prompts is not entirely new, the focus on *discriminative* concepts, guided by inter-class differences, is a strong differentiator. The combination of prior concept generation *and* test-time likelihood refinement is another unique aspect.

* **Significance:** Zero-shot learning is a critical area for real-world deployment of image recognition systems.  VLMs have shown promise, but often struggle with prompt engineering and adaptation to target classes. CHBR directly addresses these limitations by making the models more flexible and adaptable. The consistent performance gains across a diverse set of datasets suggests that CHBR is a robust approach with practical implications.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components.

* **Strengths:**
    * Strong conceptual grounding in human cognition.
    *  A well-defined probabilistic framework (Bayesian Reasoning).
    * Effective importance sampling algorithm leveraging LLMs for discriminative concept generation.
    * Test-time refinement techniques that allow for adaptation to specific images.
    * Extensive experimental validation across fifteen datasets, including fine-grained classification and domain shift scenarios.
    * A careful analysis of the contribution of different components through ablation studies.
    * The code is promised to be publicly available.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on LLMs:** The framework heavily relies on the performance and biases of the LLM (GPT-4 mini in this case).  The quality of generated concepts is crucial, and future research should explore more robust or less LLM-dependent methods.
    * **Computational Cost:** While the authors discuss efforts to reduce computational cost, the concept generation process is still time-consuming, especially when compared to a simple CLIP inference.  Scaling to even larger datasets or more complex models could be a challenge. The inference time of TTA Likelihood is also a weakness.
    * **Limited Theoretical Justification for Heuristics:** The Average Likelihood, Confidence Likelihood, and TTA Likelihood are presented as heuristics, and while they work well in practice, a deeper theoretical understanding of why these techniques are effective would strengthen the paper.
    * **Limited Exploration of Prompt Engineering for Concept Generation:** While the paper focuses on generating *discriminative* concepts, it could further explore different prompting strategies for the LLMs to improve the quality and diversity of generated concepts.  The prompts used for prompting the LLMs (shown in the appendix) appear fairly basic; more sophisticated prompting strategies could yield better results.
   * **Limited comparison to more recent prompt engineering strategies:** The baselines are strong, but a comparison to more recent, sophisticated prompt engineering strategies (besides the basic CLIP+E) would be beneficial.
   * **Lack of Error Analysis:** A deeper analysis of the types of errors that CHBR still makes (failure cases) and the reasons for those failures would provide valuable insights for future research.

* **Potential Influence:**  CHBR has the potential to influence the field of zero-shot image recognition by providing a more robust and adaptive approach. It could inspire further research into incorporating cognitive principles into VLMs and using LLMs for concept generation in a more targeted way. The framework also has the potential to be extended to other vision-language tasks, such as image captioning or visual question answering.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the paper presents a strong contribution to the field. The core idea is novel and grounded in cognitive principles, the framework is well-defined, and the experimental results are convincing. While the reliance on LLMs and computational cost are valid concerns, the authors acknowledge these limitations and suggest potential directions for future research.  The limitations listed don't invalidate the work but provide clear directions for improvement.

Score: 8

Rationale: The paper demonstrates significant novelty in integrating human-like concept reasoning for zero-shot learning within VLM settings. It provides empirical evidence and a structured methodology that advances the existing state-of-the-art by enabling adaptability and discriminative concept generation. However, it is not a perfect score due to reliance on LLMs and computational expense. Furthermore, a stronger theoretical underpinning for heuristics and deeper failure case analysis would strengthen the paper's significance.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Parameters vs. Context: Fine-Grained Control of Knowledge Reliance in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15888v1)
- **Authors**: Baolong Bi, Shenghua Liu, Yiwei Wang, Yilong Xu, Junfeng Fang, Lingrui Mei, Xueqi Cheng
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) mitigates hallucinations in Large Language Models (LLMs) by integrating external knowledge. However, conflicts between parametric knowledge and retrieved context pose challenges, particularly when retrieved information is unreliable or the model's internal knowledge is outdated. In such cases, LLMs struggle to determine whether to rely more on their own parameters or the conflicted context. To address this, we propose **CK-PLUG**, a plug-and-play method for controlling LLMs' reliance on parametric and contextual knowledge. We introduce a novel knowledge consistency metric, Confidence Gain, which detects knowledge conflicts by measuring entropy shifts in token probability distributions after context insertion. CK-PLUG then enables fine-grained control over knowledge preference by adjusting the probability distribution of tokens with negative confidence gain through a single tuning parameter. Experiments demonstrate CK-PLUG's ability to significantly regulate knowledge reliance in counterfactual RAG scenarios while maintaining generation fluency and knowledge accuracy. For instance, on Llama3-8B, memory recall (MR) of RAG response can be adjusted within a broad range (9.9%-71.9%), compared to the baseline of 42.1%. Moreover, CK-PLUG supports adaptive control based on the model's confidence in both internal and external knowledge, achieving consistent performance improvements across various general RAG tasks. Our code is available at: $\href{https://github.com/byronBBL/CK-PLUG}{\text{this https URL}}$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CK-PLUG, a plug-and-play method designed to control the reliance of Large Language Models (LLMs) on parametric versus contextual knowledge within a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) framework.  It addresses the challenge of conflicting information between LLM internal knowledge and retrieved context, particularly when retrieved context is unreliable or outdated. CK-PLUG uses a novel "Confidence Gain" metric to detect knowledge conflicts by measuring entropy shifts in token probability distributions after context insertion. It then adjusts token probabilities based on this metric, favoring either parametric or contextual knowledge through a single tuning parameter. The paper presents experiments demonstrating CK-PLUG's ability to modulate knowledge reliance in counterfactual RAG scenarios, maintaining fluency and accuracy.  The framework also supports an adaptive mode that automatically balances parametric and contextual reliance based on model confidence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the Confidence Gain metric and its use to dynamically modulate token probabilities in a plug-and-play fashion during inference.  The idea of controlling knowledge reliance in RAG is not entirely new, but CK-PLUG's approach offers a more fine-grained, token-level control compared to previous methods.  Existing alignment-based methods or context faithfulness strategies are more unidirectional, lacking the flexibility offered by CK-PLUG's bidirectional adjustment. The authors also make a valid point regarding the limitations of model editing vs. direct intervention during inference.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing importance of RAG systems and the need for reliable generation in real-world applications.  The ability to control knowledge reliance has implications for trustworthiness and adaptability in scenarios where LLMs encounter outdated or potentially adversarial information. A strength of this work is the design consideration related to avoiding the need to retrain models, thus, CK-PLUG can effectively be adopted in many areas. Also, with growing concerns about interpretability and trustworthiness in AI, the explicitness with which this method allows for knowledge dependence manipulation is significant.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Fine-Grained Control:** The token-level probability modulation offers precise control over knowledge reliance.
    *   **Plug-and-Play:** The method is designed to be easily integrated with existing LLMs without retraining, increasing its practical applicability.
    *   **Adaptive Mode:**  The automated parameter selection enhances usability and reduces the need for manual tuning.
    *   **Strong Empirical Evaluation:**  The paper presents comprehensive experiments across various datasets (NQ, ConFiQA, MQuAKE, and KILT tasks) and LLMs (LLAMA2, LLAMA3, MISTRAL, QWEN), demonstrating the effectiveness of CK-PLUG.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The paper has thorough analysis that highlights strengths and weaknesses.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Entropy:** While the Confidence Gain metric is novel, it depends on entropy calculations from token probability distributions.  The paper could benefit from further justification on its robustness across different LLM architectures and vocabulary structures. How might factors beyond knowledge conflicts (e.g. inherent token uncertainty) affect the metric's efficacy?
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** Even with the adaptive mode, the single tuning parameter α could still require some level of experimentation for optimal performance across vastly different RAG tasks.
    *   **Limited Scope of Case Studies:**  While the case studies provide insight, a larger and more diverse set could further strengthen the qualitative analysis.

*   **Potential Influence:** CK-PLUG has the potential to influence the design of future RAG systems by providing a more controllable and trustworthy approach to knowledge integration. It could also inspire further research on token-level uncertainty quantification and adaptive control mechanisms.

**Rationale for Score:**

Given the above evaluation, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field. The novelty of the Confidence Gain metric and the plug-and-play nature of CK-PLUG, combined with strong empirical results, position it as a significant advancement. However, the aforementioned weaknesses regarding entropy reliance and parameter sensitivity prevent it from reaching the highest score. Therefore, a score of 7 is justified.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Time After Time: Deep-Q Effect Estimation for Interventions on When and What to do
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15890v1)
- **Authors**: Yoav Wald, Mark Goldstein, Yonathan Efroni, Wouter A. C. van Amsterdam, Rajesh Ranganath
- **Abstract**: Problems in fields such as healthcare, robotics, and finance requires reasoning about the value both of what decision or action to take and when to take it. The prevailing hope is that artificial intelligence will support such decisions by estimating the causal effect of policies such as how to treat patients or how to allocate resources over time. However, existing methods for estimating the effect of a policy struggle with \emph{irregular time}. They either discretize time, or disregard the effect of timing policies. We present a new deep-Q algorithm that estimates the effect of both when and what to do called Earliest Disagreement Q-Evaluation (EDQ). EDQ makes use of recursion for the Q-function that is compatible with flexible sequence models, such as transformers. EDQ provides accurate estimates under standard assumptions. We validate the approach through experiments on survival time and tumor growth tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of off-policy evaluation in sequential decision-making scenarios where observations and treatments occur at irregular time intervals. Existing methods struggle with irregular time by either discretizing it or ignoring its effect on policies. The authors introduce a novel deep Q-learning algorithm called Earliest Disagreement Q-Evaluation (EDQ). EDQ leverages recursion in the Q-function and is compatible with flexible sequence models like transformers. The method estimates the causal effect of both "what" and "when" to intervene. The paper provides theoretical guarantees and demonstrates EDQ's effectiveness on survival time and tumor growth tasks compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty of this paper lies in its ability to handle irregularly spaced time data within a deep Q-learning framework for off-policy evaluation. Most existing causal inference and reinforcement learning methods either discretize time, simplifying the problem but potentially losing information and requiring careful hyperparameter tuning, or struggle to scale to complex high-dimensional, sequential problems with irregular timing. The EDQ approach, by leveraging the earliest disagreement time, offers a more adaptive and potentially more efficient solution. The algorithm's compatibility with sequence models such as transformers to learn a Q function, also increases the flexibility to capture relevant structure from complex sequential medical data.
*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is of significant practical relevance, particularly in healthcare, where treatment timing is often crucial and irregular. Accurately estimating the causal effects of different treatment policies over time can have a substantial impact on improving patient outcomes. The experiments presented, though simulations, demonstrate the potential of the EDQ approach in these challenging scenarios.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretical foundation:** The paper provides theoretical justification for EDQ, proving that it is an empirical estimator of the correct policy value under standard causal assumptions. This adds confidence in the method's reliability.
    *   **Model-free approach:** EDQ is a model-free solution, which allows it to be applied to complex environments without requiring explicit modeling of the underlying dynamics.
    *   **Compatibility with expressive models:** EDQ's compatibility with sequence models like transformers enables it to handle high-dimensional, complex sequential data.
    *   **Adaptive lookahead:** EDQ's "earliest disagreement" strategy creates an adaptive lookahead in time, making it more efficient and robust.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simulation-based validation:** The experimental validation is primarily based on simulations. While the simulations are relevant, evaluation on real-world datasets would further strengthen the paper's claims. The simulations are only based on relatively limited simulated data, compared to complex real-world datasets.
    *   **Limited scope:** Although the paper explores the treatment type as a potential consideration in the EDQ framework, the algorithm and the validation experiments are focused on the timing element of the treatment. The method's scalability with various treatment types might benefit from further investigation.
    *   **Comparison set:** Comparison with additional and more sophisticated causal inference baselines from continuous time setting, will strength the empirical contribution of the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** EDQ has the potential to influence the development of more effective and efficient off-policy evaluation methods for sequential decision-making with irregular data. The idea of using the earliest disagreement time as a way to adaptively manage the lookahead in deep Q-learning could be adopted in other settings. The framework will also impact practical treatment decision-making in Healthcare, as well as the fairness in access to healthcare and medical research.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and potentially valuable method for a significant problem. The theoretical guarantees and compatibility with expressive models are strong points. However, the lack of real-world data validation and limited exploration of other treatment types, and comparison to additional baselines, constrain its immediate impact and require more exploration. It is a solid contribution that addresses a challenging problem with a novel approach, but the empirical evaluation requires further strengthening before it can be considered a major breakthrough.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CONTHER: Human-Like Contextual Robot Learning via Hindsight Experience Replay and Transformers without Expert Demonstrations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15895v1)
- **Authors**: Maria Makarova, Qian Liu, Dzmitry Tsetserukou
- **Abstract**: This paper presents CONTHER, a novel reinforcement learning algorithm designed to efficiently and rapidly train robotic agents for goal-oriented manipulation tasks and obstacle avoidance. The algorithm uses a modified replay buffer inspired by the Hindsight Experience Replay (HER) approach to artificially populate experience with successful trajectories, effectively addressing the problem of sparse reward scenarios and eliminating the need to manually collect expert demonstrations. The developed algorithm proposes a Transformer-based architecture to incorporate the context of previous states, allowing the agent to perform a deeper analysis and make decisions in a manner more akin to human learning. The effectiveness of the built-in replay buffer, which acts as an "internal demonstrator", is twofold: it accelerates learning and allows the algorithm to adapt to different tasks. Empirical data confirm the superiority of the algorithm by an average of 38.46% over other considered methods, and the most successful baseline by 28.21%, showing higher success rates and faster convergence in the point-reaching task. Since the control is performed through the robot's joints, the algorithm facilitates potential adaptation to a real robot system and construction of an obstacle avoidance task. Therefore, the algorithm has also been tested on tasks requiring following a complex dynamic trajectory and obstacle avoidance. The design of the algorithm ensures its applicability to a wide range of goal-oriented tasks, making it an easily integrated solution for real-world robotics applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CONTHER, a novel reinforcement learning algorithm for goal-oriented robot manipulation tasks. CONTHER combines a Transformer-based architecture with a modified Hindsight Experience Replay (HER) buffer. The Transformer architecture captures contextual information from past states, enabling more human-like decision making. The modified HER buffer artificially populates the experience replay buffer with successful trajectories, addressing sparse reward challenges without relying on expert demonstrations.  The algorithm is evaluated in point-reaching and complex trajectory following with obstacle avoidance tasks, showing improved performance compared to other methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the integration of the Transformer architecture for contextual learning with the HER approach for improved sample efficiency in sparse reward environments.  While Transformers and HER are individually established techniques, their combination, specifically tailored for robot manipulation and obstacle avoidance *without* expert demonstrations, presents a unique and valuable contribution. It distinguishes itself from prior art that relies on demonstrations or fails to adequately capture long-term dependencies. The design of the CONTHER architecture and the specific modifications to the HER buffer, like incorporating context, add to its novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of CONTHER lies in its potential to simplify and accelerate the training of robotic agents for complex manipulation tasks. Eliminating the need for expert demonstrations is a crucial advantage, as collecting such demonstrations is often time-consuming and expensive. The algorithm's ability to handle sparse rewards and obstacle avoidance makes it applicable to a wide range of real-world robotics applications. The improvements in success rate and convergence speed compared to baselines demonstrate practical value.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined algorithm.
    *   The integration of Transformers and HER is a logical and effective approach.
    *   Empirical results demonstrate significant improvements over baseline methods.
    *   The algorithm is tested on both point-reaching and complex trajectory following tasks, showing versatility.
    *   The explanation of algorithm design choices, especially the modification of the HER buffer and the addition of the concatenation operation in CONTHER-v.1, is insightful.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are conducted in simulation. While valuable, real-world validation is needed to fully assess the algorithm's robustness and adaptability. The "potential adaptation" mentioned in the abstract needs to be empirically proven.
    *   A more detailed analysis of the computational complexity of CONTHER compared to the baseline methods would strengthen the paper. Transformers can be computationally expensive, and this trade-off should be discussed.
    *   The obstacle avoidance experiments, while promising, could be expanded to include more complex and dynamic obstacle scenarios.
    *   While the improvement of 38.46% is good, the paper could benefit from further analysis of what this translates to in terms of dollars saved in real-world robotic training.

*   **Potential Influence:** CONTHER has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and autonomous robot learning systems. The combination of contextual learning with HER could be adopted by other researchers in the field, leading to further advancements in robot manipulation and navigation. The algorithm's ability to operate without expert demonstrations makes it accessible to a wider range of applications and users.

*   **Missing Components:** While the paper demonstrates compelling results, there are opportunities for further validation. Testing CONTHER on a physical robot would significantly enhance the paper's impact. Adding further theoretical analysis for the algorithm would improve its comprehensibility. Furthermore, an ablation study of the CONTHER architecture (Transformer + HER) would highlight its efficacy in context of the specific contributions of either algorithm.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper demonstrates a promising approach with solid empirical results, the reliance on simulation and the lack of a detailed complexity analysis limit its overall impact. The novelty is good, but the techniques are building on existing work.

### On the Limits of Applying Graph Transformers for Brain Connectome Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15902v1)
- **Authors**: Jose Lara-Rangel, Clare Heinbaugh
- **Abstract**: Brain connectomes offer detailed maps of neural connections within the brain. Recent studies have proposed novel connectome graph datasets and attempted to improve connectome classification by using graph deep learning. With recent advances demonstrating transformers' ability to model intricate relationships and outperform in various domains, this work explores their performance on the novel NeuroGraph benchmark datasets and synthetic variants derived from probabilistically removing edges to simulate noisy data. Our findings suggest that graph transformers offer no major advantage over traditional GNNs on this dataset. Furthermore, both traditional and transformer GNN models maintain accuracy even with all edges removed, suggesting that the dataset's graph structures may not significantly impact predictions. We propose further assessing NeuroGraph as a brain connectome benchmark, emphasizing the need for well-curated datasets and improved preprocessing strategies to obtain meaningful edge connections.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the performance of graph transformers (specifically Exphormer) for brain connectome classification tasks on the NeuroGraph benchmark.  Contrary to expectations, the authors find that Exphormer does not outperform traditional Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) like ResidualGCN on these tasks. Furthermore, they demonstrate that both Exphormer and ResidualGCN maintain accuracy even when edges are randomly removed from the connectome graphs, suggesting that the graph structure in NeuroGraph may not be crucial for prediction and that node features alone might be sufficient. The authors conclude that the graph structure of NeuroGraph might not be meaningful and call for further assessment of the benchmark datasets and improved preprocessing strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has some novelty in applying sparse graph transformers (Exphormer) to brain connectome classification, a domain where transformers are gaining traction. The edge-dropping experiment to assess the importance of graph structure is a worthwhile investigation. However, the negative result (transformers not outperforming GNNs, graph structure potentially unimportant) somewhat diminishes the overall impact.
*   **Significance:** The paper challenges the implicit assumption that using advanced graph deep learning techniques, particularly transformers, automatically leads to better performance on brain connectome tasks. It raises important questions about the validity of the NeuroGraph benchmark and the preprocessing steps used to generate the graph structures. This is significant because it suggests that efforts might be better directed towards improving data quality and preprocessing than towards simply applying more sophisticated models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly describes the experiments and results.
    *   The edge-dropping experiment provides a simple but effective way to assess the importance of graph structure.
    *   The authors acknowledge limitations and suggest directions for future research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper focuses on one specific graph transformer (Exphormer). While the conclusions are generalizable to other transformers, it would have been stronger to test multiple transformer architectures.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of why the graph structure might be unimportant in NeuroGraph.  Are the connections simply noisy and not biologically relevant?
    *   The suggested "alternative fMRI processing pipelines" are not clearly defined in this work, so the reader does not know exactly how to go about creating the improved datasets they suggest.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper's value lies in its critical assessment of a popular benchmark and its challenge to the assumption that graph deep learning automatically improves brain connectome classification. The experimental results are clearly presented and are insightful. However, the somewhat negative findings and the limited exploration of the reasons behind the results prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. The suggestions for improving preprocessing, while valuable, are not fully developed in the paper. Overall, it serves as a useful cautionary tale.
